30. Beverage Delivery SOP

SOP ID V1-030 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To deliver the correct beverage to the correct guest promptly, safely, and with the required
accompaniments.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders, service assistants, runners, and managers providing coverage.
RESPONSIBILITY: The delivering employee verifies the beverage and guest position. The assigned server owns
follow-up.
WHEN USED: Whenever prepared, bottled, canned, or nonalcoholic beverages are delivered.
• Beverage ticket or POS seat information
• Approved tray or carrier
• Required glassware, straws, napkins, garnishes, and coasters
• Spill-response supplies and replacement process
• Alcohol-service and age-verification status when applicable
• The drink, preparation, and seat position match the order.
• Glassware is clean, stable, and handled away from drinking surfaces.
• Alcohol is delivered only after required approval and identification checks.
• Spills, broken glass, or suspected contamination are handled immediately.
1. Compare each beverage to the ticket, modifiers, and assigned seat.
2. Inspect glassware, garnish, temperature, fill level, and visible cleanliness.
3. Use a tray or approved carrier when quantity or conditions require it.
4. Approach safely and identify the correct guest without reaching across others.
5. Place the beverage securely with the required napkin, coaster, straw, or accompaniment.
6. State the beverage when clarification is helpful, especially for similar drinks.
7. Remove empty or unwanted glassware only after confirming with the guest.
8. Ask whether anything else is needed and note refill expectations.
9. Correct an inaccurate or unacceptable beverage promptly through the proper station.
10. Report spills, breakage, delayed drinks, or alcohol concerns to the assigned server or manager.


30. Beverage Delivery SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ Correct beverage reaches the correct guest.
□ Glassware and garnish meet quality standards.
□ Tray and safe placement practices are used.
□ Alcohol-service requirements are satisfied.
□ Required accompaniments are present.
□ Errors, spills, and delays receive immediate follow-up.
• Auctioning several similar beverages with no seat reference.
• Carrying more drinks than can be controlled safely.
• Touching rims, straws, or drinking surfaces.
• Delivering alcohol before required identification is complete.
• Leaving a spill or broken-glass area without guarding it.

2. Gather share plates, utensils, sauces, or accompaniments before leaving the window.
3. Deliver hot and cold items promptly using approved carrying methods.
4. Announce the item and place it according to seat or shared-service standards.
5. Identify any allergy-controlled plate using the approved handoff method.
6. Confirm that the table has needed utensils, napkins, and space.
7. Remove unnecessary menus or items without disrupting the guests.
8. Observe first bites and check back within the restaurant timing standard.
9. Resolve missing, incorrect, or poor-quality items immediately.
10. Coordinate clearing and the next-course fire with the assigned server or expo.

32. Entree Delivery and Table Check-Back SOP

SOP ID V1-032 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To deliver entrées accurately, complete the table, and identify quality or order problems while they can
still be corrected.
APPLIES TO: Servers, food runners, service assistants, expos, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The delivering employee verifies each plate. The assigned server owns table completion and
check-back.
WHEN USED: When main-course items are delivered and shortly after guests begin eating.
• Expo ticket, table, seat, and modifier information
• Approved trays, hot-plate protection, or carrying tools
• Required condiments, steak knives, side utensils, and accompaniments
• Allergy and special-handling identification
• Refire, missing-item, and manager escalation process
• Every entrée is matched to the correct seat and modifier.
• Hot food remains hot and cold food remains properly cold during delivery.
• Allergy-controlled plates are delivered through the approved verified process.
• The check-back occurs after guests can evaluate the food but before a problem grows.
1. Review the entire ticket and identify any missing or still-working items.
2. Verify plate appearance, sides, temperature indicators, modifiers, and seat positions.
3. Gather required condiments, knives, and accompaniments before leaving expo.
4. Deliver completed plates promptly using safe carrying and placement methods.
5. Place each item at the assigned seat and identify the dish when useful.
6. Use the approved controlled handoff for any allergy-designated plate.
7. Tell the table clearly if an item is still being completed and provide an update.
8. Confirm the table has everything needed to begin eating.
9. Check back within the approved time or bite standard and ask specific quality questions.
10. Correct or escalate missing, incorrect, undercooked, overcooked, or poor-quality items immediately.

□ Allergy-designated plate receives verified delivery.
□ Incomplete table receives a clear update.
□ Check-back occurs early enough for effective recovery.
• Auctioning plates instead of using seat positions.
• Leaving expo with a known missing side and saying nothing.
• Stacking or carrying plates in a way that damages presentation.
• Asking "everything good?" while walking away.
• Delaying a refire report until the manager notices the guest is not eating.

33. Table Maintenance and Pre-Bussing SOP

SOP ID V1-033 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To keep the table clean, organized, and properly supplied throughout the meal without interrupting the
guest experience.
APPLIES TO: Servers, service assistants, food runners, bartenders serving food, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned server owns table condition. Supporting employees maintain it and communicate
unresolved needs.
WHEN USED: Continuously from seating through check presentation and departure.
• Approved trays or bus tubs used away from guests
• Clean napkins, utensils, glassware, and table supplies
• Crumb tool or approved cleaning method
• Waste, spill, and broken-glass procedures
• Course and clearing standards
• Remove only items guests have clearly finished using.
• Dirty items do not accumulate until the next course cannot be placed safely.
• Clean and dirty items are handled separately.
• Spills, broken glass, and contamination risks receive immediate controlled response.
1. Scan the table on every approach for empty items, clutter, spills, and supply needs.
2. Ask permission when it is not clear whether a guest is finished.
3. Remove empty glassware, used plates, wrappers, and unneeded service items promptly.
4. Clear from the approved side and avoid reaching across guests whenever possible.
5. Use trays or approved methods; do not scrape or stack noisily at the table.
6. Replace needed utensils, napkins, share plates, or condiments before the next course.
7. Wipe crumbs or small spills using the approved method without contaminating food.
8. Keep chairs, aisles, and the area below the table free of service hazards.
9. Coordinate with service assistants so work is completed without duplication.
10. Perform a final table reset for dessert, check presentation, or guest departure.


33. Table Maintenance and Pre-Bussing SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Table remains organized throughout service.
□ Finished items are removed with guest awareness.
□ Clean replacements arrive before they are needed.
□ Clearing methods are quiet, safe, and professional.
□ Spills and broken glass are controlled immediately.
□ Team members communicate unfinished table needs.
• Removing a plate while another guest is still eating when policy discourages it.
• Stacking dirty dishes in front of guests.
• Reaching across faces, drinks, or hot food.
• Using a dirty towel on the tabletop.
• Walking past a spill because the table belongs to another server.

34. Refill and Beverage Monitoring SOP

SOP ID V1-034 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To maintain appropriate beverage levels and offer refills without unnecessary interruption, waste, or
surprise charges.
APPLIES TO: Servers, service assistants, bartenders, and managers providing table coverage.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned server owns refill timing and pricing communication. Supporting employees follow
the same standard.
WHEN USED: Throughout the meal and before extended gaps in table contact.
• Beverage and refill policy
• Approved pitchers, carafes, trays, or replacement glassware
• Current pricing and chargeable-refill information
• Alcohol-service monitoring process
• Spill and glassware replacement procedures
• Guests are offered refills before beverages are empty when practical.
• Chargeable refills are authorized by the guest before entry.
• Fresh glassware is used when policy or sanitation conditions require it.
• Alcohol refills follow responsible-service and identification requirements.
1. Observe beverage levels during every table approach and dining-room scan.
2. Offer a refill at the restaurant trigger point without interrupting conversation unnecessarily.
3. Confirm the beverage, modifier, and whether the refill carries a charge.
4. For self-refill beverages, follow approved pitcher or carafe handling practices.
5. For replacement drinks, enter the refill and use the correct seat position.
6. Do not combine different products or pour into visibly contaminated glassware.
7. Monitor alcohol consumption and pause service when responsible-service concerns appear.
8. Deliver the refill promptly and remove empty glassware when appropriate.
9. Update the assigned server when another employee provides a refill.
10. Escalate repeated delays, spills, guest objections, or alcohol concerns to the manager.

• Waiting for guests to wave down the server.

APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders, trainers, and managers.

• The check is reviewed before the guest sees it.
• Charges, discounts, taxes, gratuities, and service fees are represented accurately.
• The check is presented discreetly without rushing the table.
• Errors are corrected through approved authorization, not hidden or improvised.

3. Verify approved discounts, coupons, gift cards, comps, taxes, gratuities, and service charges.
4. Resolve open items, duplicate charges, or missing approvals before printing or sending.
5. Place the check in the approved presenter or activate the approved digital method.
6. Present it discreetly to the requesting guest or agreed payer.
7. Explain unusual charges, service fees, or split arrangements when needed.
8. Remain available without hovering or signaling that the table must leave.
9. Correct a questioned charge promptly and involve the manager when authorization is required.
10. Continue refills, clearing, and farewell service until the table departs.

□ Service continues after check presentation.
• Dropping the check without reviewing it.
• Presenting the check before the table is ready.

38. Payment Collection SOP

SOP ID V1-038 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To receive and process guest payment promptly while maintaining custody, accuracy, privacy, and
respectful communication.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders, cashiers, managers, and authorized payment-support employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee accepting payment maintains control until it is processed and returned.
Managers resolve exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever cash, card, gift card, mobile, or other approved payment is presented.
• Correct guest check and payment amount
• Approved POS, payment terminal, or mobile device
• Check presenter, receipt, and writing instrument
• Cash, card, gift card, and digital-payment procedures
• Manager escalation process for declines, disputes, or equipment failure
• Payment is matched to the correct check before processing.
• Cash, cards, devices, and receipts are never left unsecured.
• Guests are informed before any unusual manual or alternate process is used.
• Sensitive payment information is handled only through approved systems.
1. Acknowledge the payment promptly and confirm which check or amount it covers.
2. Keep payment inside the approved presenter or controlled method while transporting it.
3. Separate multiple payments and label their intended amounts before processing.
4. Use the correct tender key and approved device for each payment type.
5. Remain with a guest-owned mobile device; do not take it out of the guest's control.
6. Follow the specific cash, card, gift card, or split-payment SOP as applicable.
7. Verify approval, amount, balance due, and receipt before leaving the terminal.
8. Return card, change, gift card, and receipts promptly to the correct guest.
9. Handle declines or discrepancies discreetly and request another form without accusation.
10. Notify the manager of repeated failures, disputed amounts, suspected fraud, or lost payment.


38. Payment Collection SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ Payment is applied to the correct check and amount.
□ Payment remains secure from collection through return.
□ Approved terminal and tender procedures are used.
□ Receipt, change, card, and remaining balance are accurate.
□ Declines and disputes are handled discreetly.
□ Exceptions are escalated and documented when required.
• Carrying loose cards or cash without a presenter.
• Combining multiple payments before labeling their amounts.
• Taking a guest's phone away from the table.
• Leaving processed cards or change at the terminal.
• Announcing a declined payment loudly.

39. Cash Payment Handling by Servers SOP

SOP ID V1-039 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To process cash payments accurately and protect guest funds, restaurant funds, and employee
accountability.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders, cashiers, trainers, and managers authorized to handle cash.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiving employee maintains custody and records the tender. Managers approve
corrections and investigate shortages.
WHEN USED: For every cash payment, change request, cash tip adjustment, or cash-payment correction.
• Correct guest check
• Assigned bank or approved cash drawer
• POS cash-tender function
• Check presenter and receipt
• Counterfeit-concern and manager escalation procedure
• Cash is counted and secured immediately after receipt.
• The amount received is entered before change is removed from the bank.
• Restaurant funds are not mixed with personal money.
• Suspected counterfeit currency or disputes are referred to a manager without confrontation.
1. Confirm the payment applies to the correct table, check, and amount.
2. Keep the cash visible within the presenter until the amount received is identified.
3. State or note the amount received when restaurant policy requires it.
4. Enter the exact cash tender in the POS before calculating or removing change.
5. Count change from the approved bank or drawer without using personal funds.
6. Recount bills and coins before leaving the payment area.
7. Return change and receipt promptly in the presenter to the correct guest.
8. Secure retained cash in the approved bank or drawer immediately.
9. Correct a tender error only through the approved void, reopen, or manager process.
10. Report shortages, overages, disputed amounts, or suspicious currency to the manager.


39. Cash Payment Handling by Servers SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Cash tender is applied to the correct check.
□ Amount received is entered before change is made.
□ Change and receipt are accurate.
□ Cash is secured in the assigned bank or drawer.
□ Personal and restaurant funds remain separate.
□ Tender errors and suspicious currency are escalated.
• Making change from memory before entering the tender.
• Placing restaurant cash in a pocket with personal money.
• Leaving cash exposed at a service station.
• Arguing with a guest about the amount handed over.
• Fixing a cash-entry error by changing another table.

40. Credit Card Payment Handling SOP

SOP ID V1-040 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To process card payments securely through approved systems while protecting guest information and
transaction accuracy.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders, cashiers, managers, and employees authorized to process cards.
RESPONSIBILITY: The processing employee controls the card and transaction. Managers handle declines,
disputes, manual entry, and corrections.
WHEN USED: Whenever a physical card, contactless card, or approved card-linked device is used.
• Approved payment terminal or POS device
• Correct check and payment amount
• Check presenter and receipt process
• Card-decline, tip, signature, and correction procedures
• Manager approval for manual entry or offline processing
• Only approved payment devices and employee credentials are used.
• Card numbers, security codes, or magnetic-stripe data are never written down or stored.
• The card is returned promptly and kept within guest view when the service model permits.
• Manual entry, offline processing, refunds, and disputed charges require approved controls.
1. Confirm the card payment applies to the correct check and amount.
2. Inspect the terminal for obvious damage or tampering and report concerns before use.
3. Insert, tap, swipe, or present the card only through the approved device.
4. Allow the guest to control contactless or tableside entry whenever the system supports it.
5. Do not copy, photograph, record, or communicate card details outside the payment system.
6. Verify the authorization result and remaining balance before finalizing.
7. For a decline, speak discreetly and request another approved payment method.
8. Return the card and receipt promptly to the correct guest.
9. Process tips, signatures, adjustments, and closeout according to the approved system.
10. Escalate manual entry, duplicate charge, lost card, dispute, or terminal failure to the manager.


40. Credit Card Payment Handling SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Approved terminal and employee credentials are used.
□ Card data is not written, photographed, or stored.
□ Authorization and amount are verified.
□ Card and receipt are returned promptly.
□ Declines are handled privately and respectfully.
□ Manual or exception transactions receive manager control.
• Writing a card number or security code on paper.
• Taking a card to an unapproved device or location.
• Running the same card repeatedly after a decline.
• Returning the wrong card to a guest.
• Using manual entry to bypass a damaged or rejected card without approval.

41. Split Checks and Separate Payments SOP

SOP ID V1-041 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To divide charges and accept separate payments accurately without delaying service or creating
unauthorized adjustments.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders, cashiers, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The server organizes charges by seat and confirms the requested split. Managers approve
reopens, transfers, and corrections.
WHEN USED: When guests request separate checks, divided items, shared-item splits, or multiple tenders.
• Accurate seat-number and item assignment
• POS split, transfer, and payment functions
• Guest instructions for shared items and amounts
• Discount, gratuity, and service-charge rules
• Manager approval process for reopened or paid-check changes
• The requested split is confirmed before payment is processed.
• Items, tax, discounts, gratuities, and service charges remain accurate.
• No paid charge is moved or changed without approved authorization.
• Payment cards and cash are clearly matched to their intended check or amount.
1. Ask how guests want the check divided before collecting payment.
2. Clarify shared items, equal splits, individual seats, and fixed payment amounts.
3. Review seat assignments and correct misplaced items before splitting.
4. Use the approved POS split function rather than estimating totals manually.
5. Verify discounts, taxes, gratuities, and service charges on each resulting check.
6. Present the separated checks and invite guests to review them.
7. Label or organize each payment with the check or amount it covers.
8. Process one payment at a time and confirm the remaining balance after each.
9. Obtain manager help before reopening, transferring, or changing a paid check.
10. Return all receipts, cards, change, and remaining balances to the correct guests.


41. Split Checks and Separate Payments SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Split method matches the guests' request.
□ Items and shared charges are assigned correctly.
□ Tax, discounts, gratuity, and fees remain accurate.
□ Payments are matched to the intended checks.
□ Paid checks are changed only with approval.
□ Final receipts and balances are returned correctly.
• Waiting until cards are collected to ask how the check should be split.
• Dividing totals manually when the POS can track items.
• Losing track of which card belongs to which check.
• Removing an automatic gratuity without authorization.
• Moving an item off a paid check to force the totals to work.

42. Gift Card Redemption SOP

SOP ID V1-042 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify and redeem gift cards accurately while protecting balances, guest privacy, and financial
controls.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders, cashiers, trainers, and managers authorized to accept gift cards.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee processes the card through the approved system. Managers resolve invalid,
damaged, or disputed balances.
WHEN USED: Whenever a physical or digital restaurant gift card is presented for payment.
• Approved gift card and POS system
• Correct guest check and payment amount
• Gift card terms, restrictions, and balance process
• Receipt and remaining-balance procedure
• Manager escalation for unreadable, expired where lawful, or disputed cards
• Gift cards are verified and redeemed only through the approved system.
• Card numbers and balances are not announced or recorded unnecessarily.
• The amount redeemed never exceeds the approved available balance.
• Manual overrides, balance transfers, and exceptions require manager authorization.
1. Confirm the card is an accepted restaurant or program gift card.
2. Ask whether the guest wants to apply a specific amount or the available balance.
3. Scan, swipe, enter, or present the card using only the approved system.
4. Verify the displayed balance and redemption amount before finalizing.
5. Apply the redemption to the correct check and confirm any remaining amount due.
6. Return the physical card when a balance remains or policy requires return.
7. Provide the receipt or approved balance information discreetly.
8. For digital cards, allow the guest to retain control of the device whenever possible.
9. Do not manually alter a balance or substitute another tender without approval.
10. Refer unreadable, inactive, disputed, or system-failed cards to the manager.


42. Gift Card Redemption SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ Card is valid for the restaurant's approved program.
□ Redemption amount matches guest instruction and available balance.
□ Gift card is applied to the correct check.
□ Remaining balance or amount due is communicated accurately.
□ Card and receipt are returned according to policy.
□ Exceptions and manual actions receive manager approval.
• Assuming every branded card is accepted at the location.
• Reading the card number or balance aloud.
• Discarding a card that still has value.
• Entering a manual discount when the gift card system fails.
• Applying the full balance when the guest requested a smaller amount.

43. Coupon and Promotion Acceptance SOP

SOP ID V1-043 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To apply coupons and promotions consistently according to approved terms while preserving guest
trust and financial control.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders, cashiers, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee verifies published terms and enters the approved offer. Managers authorize
exceptions and disputed applications.
WHEN USED: Whenever a printed, digital, loyalty, event, or limited-time offer is presented.
• Current promotion and coupon terms
• Valid dates, items, locations, and redemption limits
• Approved POS discount key or code
• Guest check and qualifying purchase details
• Manager approval and documentation process
• The offer is valid, current, and applicable to the purchase before entry.
• Only approved POS keys and authorization levels are used.
• Stacking, exclusions, limits, and gratuity treatment follow published terms.
• Guest disagreements are handled respectfully and escalated without altering records improperly.
1. Thank the guest and review the coupon or digital offer before promising acceptance.
2. Verify date, location, qualifying items, purchase threshold, and redemption limit.
3. Confirm whether the offer may be combined with other discounts or loyalty rewards.
4. Explain any clear restriction before processing payment.
5. Apply the designated POS key, code, or scan method to the correct items or check.
6. Obtain manager approval when the offer requires authorization or appears unclear.
7. Recheck tax, gratuity, service charge, and final total after application.
8. Mark, collect, or return the coupon according to program rules.
9. Document the promotion code or reason when required for audit.
10. Escalate disputed, altered, duplicated, or system-rejected offers to the manager.


43. Coupon and Promotion Acceptance SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Offer meets date, location, item, and purchase requirements.
□ Stacking and redemption limits are followed.
□ Correct POS key and approval level are used.
□ Final check reflects the intended discount.
□ Coupon disposition and documentation follow policy.
□ Disputes are escalated without arguing.
• Promising acceptance before reading the terms.
• Using a general discount key instead of the promotion code.
• Applying the offer to excluded alcohol, tax, gratuity, or service charges.
• Keeping a guest's phone to inspect a digital offer.
• Changing the expiration date or terms without authorization.

44. Server Checkout SOP

SOP ID V1-044 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To reconcile each server's sales, payments, tips, cash due, open checks, and controlled items before
shift release.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders with server banks, closing leads, cashiers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The server prepares an accurate checkout. The manager verifies discrepancies, approvals,
records, and final release.
WHEN USED: At shift end, server cut, role transfer, or emergency departure.
• POS checkout and sales report
• Assigned bank, cash, receipts, and payment records
• Open-check, void, comp, discount, and transfer report
• Tip declaration or adjustment process
• Side-work sign-off and controlled-item return list
• No server leaves with open checks, unreconciled payments, or unreturned controlled items.
• Cash due and tip records are calculated through the approved system.
• Voids, comps, transfers, discounts, and corrections have proper authorization.
• Checkout records and payment information are secured according to policy.
1. Complete active tables or transfer them with manager and receiving-server approval.
2. Review the POS for open, unpaid, duplicate, or incorrectly assigned checks.
3. Confirm all card payments, tips, gift cards, discounts, comps, and voids are finalized.
4. Print or generate the approved checkout and sales report.
5. Count the assigned bank and separate restaurant cash due from employee tips.
6. Prepare cash due, receipts, vouchers, and required records in the approved order.
7. Return handhelds, keys, pagers, banks, and other controlled items.
8. Complete closing or cut side work and obtain the required sign-off.
9. Present the checkout to the manager and resolve any shortage, overage, or exception.
10. Leave only after the manager approves the checkout and releases the employee.


44. Server Checkout SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ All tables and checks are closed or formally transferred.
□ Payments and tips are finalized in the POS.
□ Cash due matches the approved checkout.
□ Voids, comps, discounts, and transfers are authorized.
□ Side work and controlled items are complete.
□ Manager documents discrepancies and approves release.
• Printing the report before finalizing all payments.
• Using another employee's cash or tips to cover a shortage.
• Leaving open checks for the closing server without transfer.
• Discarding receipts or payment records prematurely.
• Leaving after dropping off cash without manager approval.

• Menu, POS, seat-number, payment, or check-handling system: _______________________________________

45. Server Closing Side Work SOP

SOP ID V1-045 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To clean, restock, reset, and secure server areas so the restaurant is ready for the next operating
period.
APPLIES TO: Closing servers, assigned side-work employees, shift leads, and closing managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Each server completes assigned work without reducing active guest service. The manager
verifies before release.
WHEN USED: During controlled closing periods and after the final assigned guest departs.
• Closing side-work chart or checklist
• Approved cleaning and sanitizing supplies
• Restocking pars and storage locations
• Trash, recycling, and linen procedures
• Equipment shutdown and security instructions
• Guest service continues until the manager releases the employee or area.
• Food-contact and guest-contact surfaces are cleaned with approved methods.
• Supplies are restocked to the next-shift par without hiding shortages.
• Equipment, cash areas, records, and access points are secured.
1. Review the assigned closing work and confirm which tasks can begin during service.
2. Maintain table coverage and obtain permission before leaving the section for side work.
3. Remove trash, used linen, clutter, and unneeded service items from assigned areas.
4. Clean and sanitize approved stations, shelves, trays, menus, and touchpoints.
5. Restock napkins, silverware, condiments, paper goods, and other supplies to par.
6. Clean and reset tables, chairs, booths, floors, and service stations.
7. Return food, beverages, chemicals, and equipment to approved storage locations.
8. Shut down or secure assigned devices only after manager approval.
9. Report shortages, breakage, maintenance needs, pest evidence, or incomplete shared work.
10. Complete the checklist and obtain manager verification before checkout and release.


45. Server Closing Side Work SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ Active guests continue receiving full service.
□ Assigned stations and section are clean and reset.
□ Supplies are restocked to the approved par.
□ Waste, linen, food, and chemicals are handled correctly.
□ Equipment and controlled areas are secured.
□ Shortages and maintenance concerns are documented.
• Starting noisy cleaning around guests too early.
• Leaving the section unattended to complete side work.
• Using spray chemicals near food or clean tableware.
• Filling containers without cleaning or rotating them as required.
• Signing off shared tasks without inspecting them.

46. Server Shift Handoff SOP

SOP ID V1-046 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To transfer active tables, checks, guest needs, and section responsibilities without loss of service or
accountability.
APPLIES TO: Servers, relief servers, bartenders covering tables, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The outgoing server gives a complete handoff. The incoming server verifies and accepts each
responsibility with manager awareness.
WHEN USED: At breaks, cuts, shift changes, section changes, or emergency coverage transfers.
• Current table and seat information
• Open checks and payment status
• Course timing, allergy, celebration, and complaint notes
• Section side-work and refill needs
• POS transfer function and manager approval
• No active table is transferred without a named receiving employee.
• Allergy concerns, unpaid checks, complaints, and time-sensitive needs are stated explicitly.
• POS ownership and verbal ownership agree before the outgoing server leaves.
• Guest privacy is protected during the handoff.
1. Notify the manager or shift lead that a handoff is needed.
2. Identify every active, recently closed, and reserved table in the section.
3. Review each table's course, open items, refills, timing, and special requests.
4. State allergy alerts, guest complaints, recovery actions, and manager follow-up separately.
5. Review checks, payments in progress, split arrangements, and gift cards or coupons.
6. Transfer tables in the POS using the approved function and authorization.
7. Introduce the incoming server to guests when practical and appropriate.
8. The incoming server repeats critical information and checks the section in person.
9. Document unresolved issues, side work, equipment, or supply needs.
10. The outgoing server leaves only after acceptance and manager release.


46. Server Shift Handoff SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ Every active table has an identified receiving server.
□ Course, refill, and open-item status are transferred.
□ Allergy and complaint information is stated clearly.
□ Open checks and payment custody are controlled.
□ POS ownership matches actual service ownership.
□ Manager approves the completed handoff.
• Saying "they are fine" instead of reviewing each table.
• Leaving a card, cash payment, or open check without direct transfer.
• Failing to identify an allergy order or unresolved complaint.
• Transferring the POS check but not greeting the guests.
• Leaving before the incoming server verifies the section.

47. Section Reset Between Seatings SOP

SOP ID V1-047 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To return a vacated table and surrounding area to a clean, complete, and approved guest-ready
condition.
APPLIES TO: Servers, service assistants, hosts when assigned, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned section team completes the reset. The server or manager verifies readiness
before the table is marked available.
WHEN USED: After guest departure and before the next seating.
• Approved bus tub, tray, or dish-removal method
• Cleaning and sanitizing supplies
• Clean tableware, menus, condiments, and settings
• Floor and spill-response tools
• Table-status communication system
• Dirty items are removed before clean settings are placed.
• Tabletop, seating, and high-touch areas are cleaned using approved methods.
• The floor and guest path are safe before the table is released.
• The table is marked available only after the reset is complete.
1. Thank departing guests and wait until personal items are removed.
2. Check seats, floor, and tabletop for lost property before clearing.
3. Remove all dirty dishes, glassware, linen, trash, and used condiments.
4. Separate and transport dirty items using the approved route and container.
5. Clean and sanitize the tabletop and required high-touch surfaces.
6. Inspect and correct chairs, booths, table stability, and surrounding floor.
7. Replace clean settings, menus, condiments, and required table supplies.
8. Align the table and chairs to the approved layout without blocking access.
9. Perform a final scan for residue, damage, odor, or missing items.
10. Update the host or table-status system only when the table is fully ready.


47. Section Reset Between Seatings SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Dirty items are removed before resetting.
□ Tabletop and seating meet cleaning standards.
□ Floor and access path are safe.
□ Settings and supplies are complete and correctly placed.
□ Lost property and damage are reported.
□ Table status changes only after final verification.
• Placing clean silverware while dirty dishes remain.
• Wiping crumbs onto chairs or the floor.
• Marking the table open before the floor is safe.
• Reusing a visibly soiled menu or condiment container.
• Missing a phone, wallet, or other guest property under the table.

48. Dining Room Readiness During Service SOP

SOP ID V1-048 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To maintain a clean, safe, comfortable, and fully functioning dining room throughout operating hours.
APPLIES TO: Servers, service assistants, hosts, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Every employee corrects or reports visible concerns. The manager coordinates priorities that
affect guests or safety.
WHEN USED: Continuously during service, especially before rushes, shift changes, and large-party arrivals.
• Dining-room zone or inspection plan
• Approved cleaning and spill-response supplies
• Table-status and maintenance communication method
• Lighting, temperature, music, and facility standards
• Restroom, entry, and emergency-path inspection process
• Guest paths, exits, and accessible routes remain clear and safe.
• Cleaning is performed discreetly without contaminating food or disrupting guests.
• Lighting, temperature, sound, odor, and visible condition support the concept standard.
• Safety hazards and equipment failures receive immediate control and escalation.
1. Scan the assigned zone on every pass rather than focusing only on active tables.
2. Correct clutter, empty glassware, dirty tables, and misplaced furniture promptly.
3. Guard and correct spills, broken glass, loose mats, or trip hazards immediately.
4. Inspect floors, walls, windows, fixtures, menus, and visible service stations.
5. Confirm aisles, exits, highchairs, mobility routes, and service paths remain clear.
6. Monitor lighting, music, temperature, odor, and guest comfort; report concerns.
7. Coordinate discreet table resets and cleaning around occupied areas.
8. Check restroom and entry readiness according to assigned frequency.
9. Report damaged furniture, pests, leaks, equipment failure, or repeated cleanliness issues.
10. Record required checks and confirm corrections with the shift lead or manager.


48. Dining Room Readiness During Service SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Guest paths and exits are clear.
□ Tables, stations, floors, and visible surfaces remain presentable.
□ Spills and hazards receive immediate control.
□ Comfort conditions meet the restaurant standard.
□ Restroom and entry checks occur as assigned.
□ Facility and maintenance issues are documented and escalated.
• Walking past a hazard because it is outside the assigned section.
• Spraying chemicals near guests, food, or clean settings.
• Allowing dirty empty tables to remain visible during a wait.
• Ignoring uncomfortable temperature, lighting, or music levels.
• Reporting a problem without confirming that someone owns the correction.

49. Guest Allergy Communication by Servers SOP

SOP ID V1-049 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To communicate a guest-reported food allergy accurately from the table through preparation and
verified delivery.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders taking food orders, trainers, managers, expo, and designated kitchen leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The server captures the guest's exact concern and initiates the approved alert. The manager
and kitchen determine available options and required controls.
WHEN USED: Whenever a guest reports an allergy, allergen avoidance need, or concern about ingredients or
cross-contact.
• Current ingredient and allergen reference
• Approved POS allergy alert and modifier keys
• Manager and kitchen communication channel
• Allergy-order identification and delivery method
• Emergency-response procedure for a suspected reaction
• Every allergy statement is treated seriously and communicated without delay.
• Servers never guess, guarantee an item is allergen-free, or rely only on memory.
• Allergy alerts are both entered in the POS and verbally acknowledged as policy requires.
• The correct identified meal is delivered directly to the correct guest through the approved handoff.
1. Stop ordinary order entry and listen to the guest's exact allergen concern.
2. Repeat the allergen back and clarify the item or ingredients the guest must avoid.
3. Tell the guest that the manager or kitchen must verify available options.
4. Notify the manager and designated kitchen contact using the approved alert words.
5. Consult only current approved ingredient information; do not infer from item names.
6. Enter the allergy alert, item, modifiers, and seat using designated POS keys.
7. Obtain verbal acknowledgment from the responsible kitchen or expo employee.
8. Do not change, add, or substitute ingredients after approval without restarting verification.
9. Verify the identified plate and deliver it directly through the approved controlled handoff.
10. Check back promptly; if symptoms are reported, summon the manager and activate the emergency-response SOP
immediately.


49. Guest Allergy Communication by Servers SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Guest's allergen concern is repeated and recorded accurately.
□ Manager and kitchen receive both required POS and verbal notice.
□ Approved ingredient information is used.
□ No unsupported safety guarantee is made.
□ Plate and guest identity are verified at delivery.
□ Suspected reaction triggers immediate emergency response.
• Treating an allergy as an ordinary preference modifier.
• Saying an item is safe because it has been served before.
• Entering a note but failing to notify the kitchen verbally.
• Allowing an unapproved substitution after the order is verified.
• Auctioning an allergy-designated plate at the table.

• Menu, POS, seat-number, payment, or check-handling system: _______________________________________
• Allergy, special-request, recovery, or manager escalation role: __________________________________
• Greeting, firing, check-back, refill, payment, or reset target: ___________________________________
• Payment-security, accessibility, alcohol, or local service requirement reviewed: __________________

50. Server Guest Complaint Escalation SOP

SOP ID V1-050 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To recognize complaints that require leadership involvement and transfer them to a manager without
delay, argument, or loss of facts.
APPLIES TO: Servers, bartenders, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee listens, stabilizes immediate service, and contacts the manager. The manager
owns investigation, authorization, and follow-up.
WHEN USED: Whenever a guest expresses dissatisfaction beyond the employee's authority or raises a safety,
illness, injury, allergy, conduct, or payment concern.
• Manager contact and escalation method
• Service-recovery authority limits
• Guest complaint or incident documentation form
• Check, ticket, item, and employee information
• Emergency and safety response procedures
• Safety, illness, injury, allergy, harassment, threat, and payment-security concerns are escalated immediately.
• Employees listen without arguing, blaming, or making unauthorized promises.
• The manager receives the guest's exact concern and actions already taken.
• Relevant food, objects, checks, receipts, or records are preserved according to policy.
1. Stop, make eye contact, and allow the guest to explain without interruption.
2. Thank the guest for telling you and acknowledge the experience professionally.
3. Clarify the requested outcome and determine whether an immediate safety issue exists.
4. Correct a simple service need only within your approved authority.
5. Tell the guest when manager involvement is needed and contact the manager promptly.
6. Give the manager the table, guest, exact concern, timing, item, and actions already taken.
7. Remain available and do not debate fault, policy, or compensation with the guest.
8. Preserve relevant food, foreign objects, receipts, or transaction details when instructed.
9. Follow through on assigned corrections and check back after the manager response.
10. Document required facts before the shift ends and report any unresolved concern.


50. Server Guest Complaint Escalation SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Employee listens and acknowledges the complaint professionally.
□ Immediate safety or emergency risks are identified.
□ Manager is contacted within the approved escalation time.
□ Facts and prior actions are transferred accurately.
□ Employee stays within recovery and compensation authority.
□ Required evidence and documentation are preserved.
• Defending the restaurant before understanding the concern.
• Promising a refund, comp, firing, or other outcome without authority.
• Waiting until checkout to mention a serious complaint.
• Summarizing the concern vaguely and omitting what was already promised.
• Discarding the food, object, receipt, or record involved in the complaint.
• Approved service language, sequence, or table standard: __________________________________________
• Menu, POS, seat-number, payment, or check-handling system: _______________________________________
• Allergy, special-request, recovery, or manager escalation role: __________________________________
• Greeting, firing, check-back, refill, payment, or reset target: ___________________________________
• Payment-security, accessibility, alcohol, or local service requirement reviewed: __________________
• Concept-specific menu, course, section, or side-work adjustments: _________________________________

PART 3

SERVICE ASSISTANTS, FOOD RUNNING,
TO-GO & DELIVERY

20 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control food handoffs, table support, resetting, takeout accuracy, packaging, curbside service,
delivery partners, and station continuity.


51. Food Running SOP

SOP ID V1-051 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To deliver finished food from expo to the correct table and seat quickly while protecting accuracy,
temperature, presentation, and allergy controls.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever food running is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• Required accompaniments or replacement items
• Server, expo, or manager communication
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for food running.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Confirm the table, seat positions, item count, course, and allergy or special-order identifiers with expo.
5. Compare the ticket to every plate and verify required sauces, garnishes, and accompaniments.
6. Carry food by the approved method and deliver each item to the identified seat without auctioning or reaching across guests.
7. Return mismatched, incomplete, unsafe, or questionable food to expo immediately and update the server.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

CUSTOMIZATION

□ Confirm the table, seat positions, item count, course, and allergy or special-order identifiers with expo.

guests.
□ Return mismatched, incomplete, unsafe, or questionable food to expo immediately and update the server.
□ Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
• Beginning food running without confirming the guest, table, seat, item, timing, and responsible employee.
• Skipping an allergy, temperature, quality, identity, readiness, or safe-handling check to save time.
• Using an unsafe route, overloaded tray, unapproved handling method, or intrusive guest approach.
• Failing to communicate a delay, rejected item, missing need, or completed handoff promptly.
• Resetting the area or moving to another task while a guest need or exception remains unassigned.

52. Expo-to-Runner Handoff SOP

SOP ID V1-052 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To transfer complete ticket, table, seat, timing, allergy, and quality information from expo to the
assigned food runner.
APPLIES TO: Employees releasing or receiving the order, station, transaction, or responsibility, plus managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The releasing employee gives complete information. The receiving employee confirms
acceptance; management resolves exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever responsibility, custody, information, or an active order transfers between employees.
• Current order, guest, or work status
• Approved handoff record
• Identity, table, seat, or transaction information
• Manager communication
• Exception documentation
• The handoff is direct, complete, and acknowledged.
• Allergy, cash, guest, safety, identity, and timing information is repeated back when relevant.
• Responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.
• Every unresolved item has a named owner, deadline, and manager awareness.
1. Identify the exact order, guest, station, transaction, custody, and time period covered by expo-to-runner handoff.
2. Conduct the transfer directly whenever possible and use the approved written or electronic record.
3. Review active work, deadlines, high-risk conditions, and items awaiting manager action.
4. Expo identifies table, seat numbers, item count, course, allergy flags, and special handling before release.
5. The runner repeats back critical information and visually compares the ticket to the completed plates.
6. Both employees confirm sauces, sides, garnishes, and missing items before the runner lifts the order.
7. The runner reports delivery, table-access problems, or rejected items so expo can close the handoff loop.
8. Require repeat-back of critical allergy, cash, identity, safety, timing, and guest information.
9. Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
10. Both employees acknowledge the transfer; responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.


52. Expo-to-Runner Handoff SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the correct guest, table, order, service trigger, and manager escalation point for expo-to-
runner handoff.
□ Expo identifies table, seat numbers, item count, course, allergy flags, and special handling before release.
□ The runner repeats back critical information and visually compares the ticket to the completed plates.
□ Both employees confirm sauces, sides, garnishes, and missing items before the runner lifts the order.
□ The runner reports delivery, table-access problems, or rejected items so expo can close the handoff loop.
□ Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
• Giving a general verbal update instead of exact identifiers and current status for expo-to-runner handoff.
• Failing to repeat back allergy, identity, cash, timing, or safety information.
• Assuming the receiver accepted responsibility without clear acknowledgment.
• Omitting an unresolved item, named owner, deadline, or manager notification.
• Discarding the source ticket, log, or record before the transfer is verified.

53. Seat-Number and Position Delivery SOP

SOP ID V1-053 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To use seat numbers and table positions so each item reaches the correct guest without auctioning
food or losing allergy control.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever seat-number and position delivery is required during opening, service, shift transition, or
closing.
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• Required accompaniments or replacement items
• Server, expo, or manager communication
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for seat-number and position delivery.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Identify seat one and the approved clockwise or position sequence before approaching the table.
5. Arrange items in delivery order and match every item to the ticket seat number.
6. Announce items discreetly and confirm uncertain positions with the server rather than auctioning food.
7. Report moved guests or changed seats so the POS, server, and expo information can be corrected.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

54. Hot Food Delivery Priority SOP

SOP ID V1-054 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prioritize and deliver hot food immediately so temperature, presentation, ticket completeness, and
guest timing are protected.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever hot food delivery priority is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• Required accompaniments or replacement items
• Server, expo, or manager communication
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Review the expo screen or ticket rail continuously and identify hot entrées, sides, and sauces that are fully assembled and
released for delivery.
2. Confirm the table number, seat positions, item names, modifiers, allergy indicators, and required accompaniments before
touching the plates.
3. Give immediate priority to released hot food; pause lower-priority side work and request runner assistance when more plates
are ready than one person can carry safely.
4. Use clean, dry service cloths or approved trays and handle plates only by safe, non-food-contact surfaces.
5. Deliver all available items for the table together when practical, while never holding a completed hot plate solely to wait for a
delayed item without manager or expo direction.
6. Announce each item clearly at the correct seat and verify special modifications discreetly without describing a guest's medical
information.
7. Place plates safely, keep hands and clothing away from food-contact areas, and warn guests about unusually hot plates when
appropriate.
8. Notify the server and expo immediately about missing items, incorrect temperatures, damaged presentation, or a guest who
questions the order.
9. Return rejected or incorrect food through the approved correction process; never relocate garnish, sauce, or food between
guest plates.
10. Clear the delivery path, confirm the table has necessary utensils and condiments, and communicate any unresolved need
before leaving.


54. Hot Food Delivery Priority SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the correct guest, table, order, service trigger, and manager escalation point for hot food
delivery priority.
□ Confirm the table number, seat positions, item names, modifiers, allergy indicators, and required accompaniments
before touching the plates.
□ Use clean, dry service cloths or approved trays and handle plates only by safe, non-food-contact surfaces.
□ Announce each item clearly at the correct seat and verify special modifications discreetly without describing a guest's
medical information.
□ Notify the server and expo immediately about missing items, incorrect temperatures, damaged presentation, or a guest
who questions the order.
□ Clear the delivery path, confirm the table has necessary utensils and condiments, and communicate any unresolved
need before leaving.
• Allowing released hot food to sit while completing side work or waiting unnecessarily for another item.
• Delivering to the wrong seat or missing modifiers, allergy indicators, sauces, or accompaniments.
• Carrying unsafe plate quantities, touching food-contact surfaces, or using soiled service cloths.
• Delivering visibly incorrect, cooling, damaged, or incomplete food instead of returning it to expo.
• Failing to tell the server or expo about delayed, rejected, or missing items.

55. Cold Food Delivery Priority SOP

SOP ID V1-055 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect cold-food temperature, presentation, and order accuracy while coordinating delivery with the
rest of the table.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever cold food delivery priority is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• Required accompaniments or replacement items
• Server, expo, or manager communication
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Monitor the expo area for released salads, chilled appetizers, desserts, cold sides, and other temperature-sensitive cold items.
2. Match each item to the ticket, table, seat, modifier, allergy indicator, garnish, dressing, and required service piece before
delivery.
3. Deliver released cold food promptly and keep it away from heat lamps, hot plates, steam, direct sunlight, and other warming
sources.
4. Use clean trays or approved carrying methods and protect chilled food from bare-hand contact, cross-contact, spills, and
stacked plate damage.
5. Coordinate with expo when hot and cold courses must arrive together, but do not allow cold food to warm while waiting without
manager direction.
6. Announce each item at the correct seat and confirm dressings, sauces, chilled accompaniments, and special modifications.
7. Observe appearance and temperature; return wilted, melting, warm, damaged, or incorrectly garnished items before they
reach the guest.
8. Notify the server immediately when an item is delivered separately, delayed, corrected, or refused.
9. Handle returned or questioned cold food through the approved remake and discard process; never re-serve food that reached
a guest.
10. Confirm the guest has the correct utensils and accompaniments, then clear the pickup area so the next cold item remains
protected.


55. Cold Food Delivery Priority SOP - VERIFICATION
& CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the correct guest, table, order, service trigger, and manager escalation point for cold food
delivery priority.
□ Match each item to the ticket, table, seat, modifier, allergy indicator, garnish, dressing, and required service piece
before delivery.
□ Use clean trays or approved carrying methods and protect chilled food from bare-hand contact, cross-contact, spills,
and stacked plate damage.
□ Announce each item at the correct seat and confirm dressings, sauces, chilled accompaniments, and special
modifications.
□ Notify the server immediately when an item is delivered separately, delayed, corrected, or refused.
□ Confirm the guest has the correct utensils and accompaniments, then clear the pickup area so the next cold item
remains protected.
• Leaving chilled food under heat lamps or near hot equipment while waiting for delivery.
• Missing dressings, garnishes, modifiers, allergy indicators, or required chilled accompaniments.
• Delivering wilted, melting, warm, spilled, or presentation-damaged food.
• Holding cold items too long solely to synchronize a course without expo or manager direction.
• Re-serving or rearranging food that has already reached a guest.

56. Table Clearing and Pre-Bussing SOP

SOP ID V1-056 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To remove used items throughout the meal without interrupting guests, clearing needed items, or
creating sanitation and safety hazards.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever table clearing and pre-bussing is required during opening, service, shift transition, or
closing.
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• Required accompaniments or replacement items
• Server, expo, or manager communication
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for table clearing and pre-bussing.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Observe the entire table and confirm guests are finished before removing shared or individual items.
5. Ask permission when intent is unclear and avoid interrupting active conversation or reaching across guests.
6. Separate sharp, hot, liquid, and breakable items and avoid overloading hands, trays, or bus tubs.
7. Replace needed utensils or space and tell the server about spills, guest needs, or course readiness.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

57. Table Reset SOP

SOP ID V1-057 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To return each vacated table and surrounding area to a clean, complete, stable, and approved guest-
ready condition.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever table reset is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• Required accompaniments or replacement items
• Server, expo, or manager communication
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for table reset.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Remove all dishes, trash, receipts, and guest property and route found items through lost-and-found control.
5. Clean and sanitize the tabletop and required touchpoints using approved chemicals and contact time.
6. Inspect chairs, booths, floors, table stability, and the surrounding area for cleanliness and safety.
7. Replace menus, settings, condiments, and table items in the approved arrangement and mark the table ready.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

58. Water Service SOP

SOP ID V1-058 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To provide timely, sanitary water service while respecting restaurant policy, guest preference, table
awareness, and spill prevention.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.

3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Use clean approved glassware and the restaurant standard for automatic, filtered, bottled, or request-only water.
5. Pour without touching the pitcher or bottle to the glass rim and avoid overfilling or splashing.
6. Monitor water during table visits and refill at the approved point while respecting guests who decline.

• Required accompaniments or replacement items
• Server, expo, or manager communication
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for bread or complimentary item service.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Confirm portion, refill, charge, timing, allergy, and outside-item rules before service.
5. Prepare or retrieve the item with clean utensils and approved portioning and holding methods.
6. Deliver required plates, spreads, sauces, or explanations and communicate known allergen concerns.
7. Monitor refills and record unusual waste, repeated requests, or guest concerns.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

60. Service Assistant Opening Duties SOP

SOP ID V1-060 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prepare service-assistant stations, supplies, routes, and shared duties before guests arrive and
obtain manager release for service.
APPLIES TO: Assigned opening employees, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Assigned employees complete the opening work. The manager or shift lead verifies readiness
before service begins.
WHEN USED: Before the assigned station, department, or building begins service or changes opening
responsibility.
• Current opening checklist
• Approved cleaning and sanitizing supplies
• Required stock and operating tools
• System, equipment, cash, or access information
• Manager communication
• The area is clean, stocked, safe, and operational before service begins.
• Cash, product, keys, systems, and equipment are verified before use.
• Shortages, damage, hazards, and incomplete work are reported before guests are affected.
• Opening is not signed off until management verifies readiness.
1. Review the current service assistant opening duties checklist, expected business, assignments, and manager notes.
2. Gather approved tools and obtain only authorized cash, keys, product, or system access.
3. Inspect the assigned area for damage, contamination, shortages, unsafe conditions, and incomplete prior work.
4. Set up or break down bus stations, tray stands, water areas, and shared service equipment according to the assignment.
5. Stock or return glassware, utensils, napkins, condiments, cleaning supplies, and support tools to approved pars.
6. Inspect dining-room floors, aisles, chairs, highchairs, and shared areas for cleanliness and hazards.
7. Confirm food-running, pre-bussing, water, reset, trash, and final manager-check responsibilities.
8. Correct and report shortages, equipment problems, unsafe conditions, or incomplete work before service.
9. Complete a final readiness check and demonstrate critical controls requested by management.
10. Record completion and obtain manager or shift-lead release before the area begins service.

61. Service Assistant Closing Duties SOP

SOP ID V1-061 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To complete service-assistant closing duties, restore shared areas, document exceptions, and transfer
unfinished work before leaving.
APPLIES TO: Assigned closing employees, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Assigned employees complete closing work. The manager verifies sanitation, cash, security,
and unresolved conditions before release.
WHEN USED: After active service ends and before employees are released or the building is secured.
• Current closing checklist
• Approved cleaning and sanitizing supplies
• Storage, waste, and security controls
• Active guest, order, or transaction status
• Manager sign-off
• All guests, transactions, product, cash, equipment, and records are resolved or transferred.
• Cleaning, storage, waste, and security follow approved standards.
• Damage, shortages, incidents, and unfinished work are documented.
• The employee leaves only after management verifies the closing assignment.
1. Review the current service assistant closing duties checklist and confirm management has approved the start of closing work.
2. Identify active guests, orders, checks, cash, controlled product, and duties that must remain available.
3. Gather approved cleaning, storage, waste, and security supplies without obstructing operating areas.
4. Set up or break down bus stations, tray stands, water areas, and shared service equipment according to the assignment.
5. Stock or return glassware, utensils, napkins, condiments, cleaning supplies, and support tools to approved pars.
6. Inspect dining-room floors, aisles, chairs, highchairs, and shared areas for cleanliness and hazards.
7. Confirm food-running, pre-bussing, water, reset, trash, and final manager-check responsibilities.
8. Report breakage, shortage, maintenance, safety, cash, or guest issues and assign unresolved follow-up.
9. Complete the final checklist and walk the area with the manager or designated verifier.
10. Sign off only after work is accepted and remaining responsibility is formally transferred.

63. To-Go Order Taking SOP

SOP ID V1-063 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To capture complete to-go orders with accurate items, modifiers, allergies, timing, guest contact
information, and payment instructions.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever to-go order taking is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Order labels, packaging, and tamper-evident supplies
• Pickup, driver, or vehicle verification information
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for to-go order taking.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Record the order channel, guest name, callback number, pickup method, requested time, and payment expectation.
5. Enter every item, quantity, side, modifier, allergy, and special instruction while the guest is communicating.
6. Read back the complete order, price, ready-time estimate, pickup location, and remaining payment steps.
7. Send only after confirmation and escalate unavailable items, unusual requests, and unresolved allergy questions.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

64. To-Go Order Accuracy Verification SOP

SOP ID V1-064 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify every to-go item, modifier, allergy note, quantity, label, payment status, and accompaniment
before release.
APPLIES TO: Assigned employees, shift leads, and managers responsible for the process.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee checks the current standard and communicates changes. The
manager authorizes exceptions and verifies correction.
WHEN USED: Whenever to-go order accuracy verification is required during opening, service, shift transition, or
closing.
• Order labels, packaging, and tamper-evident supplies
• Pickup, driver, or vehicle verification information
• Current status display, report, or checklist
• Approved timing, quality, capacity, or safety standard
• Employee and station assignments
• Status is checked against an approved timing, quality, safety, or capacity standard.
• Changes are communicated early enough for employees and guests to respond.
• Exceptions are recorded instead of relying on verbal memory.
• Management intervenes when the process exceeds authority, capacity, or safe limits.
1. Review current status, timing, staffing, volume, standards, and manager priorities relevant to verifying to-go order accuracy.
2. Observe the actual process and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify immediate hazards, delays, capacity limits, mismatches, and work without a clear owner.
4. Match guest name, order number, item count, sizes, modifiers, sides, and special instructions to the final ticket.
5. Inspect containers and labels without contaminating food or breaking required seals.
6. Confirm sauces, condiments, utensils, beverages, desserts, and paid add-ons are present.
7. Obtain required second verification for allergy, large, high-value, or other designated orders.
8. Assign corrections to named employees, communicate status, and set completion and recheck times.
9. Recheck the highest-risk items and escalate when the process exceeds authority, capacity, or safe limits.
10. Record decisions, exceptions, and follow-up for management review.

□ Match guest name, order number, item count, sizes, modifiers, sides, and special instructions to the final ticket.
□ Inspect containers and labels without contaminating food or breaking required seals.
□ Confirm sauces, condiments, utensils, beverages, desserts, and paid add-ons are present.
□ Obtain required second verification for allergy, large, high-value, or other designated orders.
□ Recheck the highest-risk items and escalate when the process exceeds authority, capacity, or safe limits.
• Reviewing to-go order accuracy verification from a screen or report without checking the actual operation.
• Using an assumed target instead of the current approved timing, quality, capacity, or safety standard.
• Waiting until the problem affects guests or employees before communicating the change.
• Assigning a correction without a named owner, deadline, and recheck.
• Closing the review while an exception remains undocumented or beyond current authority.

65. To-Go Packaging and Labeling SOP

SOP ID V1-065 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To package and label to-go orders so food quality, temperature, allergy identification, completeness,
tamper control, and guest identity are protected.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever to-go packaging and labeling is required during opening, service, shift transition, or
closing.
• Order labels, packaging, and tamper-evident supplies
• Pickup, driver, or vehicle verification information
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for to-go packaging and labeling.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Select containers that fit the item, prevent leaks, preserve temperature, and separate incompatible products.
5. Close and label containers with guest, order, item, allergy, and handling information as required.
6. Bag hot, cold, beverage, and fragile items according to the transport standard.
7. Apply tamper-evident controls and stage the order in the correct pickup or temperature-controlled area.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.
