66. Curbside Pickup SOP

SOP ID V1-066 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To release curbside orders to the verified guest or vehicle safely, promptly, accurately, and with
documented payment and handoff status.
APPLIES TO: Employees releasing or receiving the order, station, transaction, or responsibility, plus managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The releasing employee gives complete information. The receiving employee confirms
acceptance; management resolves exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever curbside pickup is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Order labels, packaging, and tamper-evident supplies
• Pickup, driver, or vehicle verification information
• Current order, guest, or work status
• Approved handoff record
• Identity, table, seat, or transaction information
• The handoff is direct, complete, and acknowledged.
• Allergy, cash, guest, safety, identity, and timing information is repeated back when relevant.
• Responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.
• Every unresolved item has a named owner, deadline, and manager awareness.
1. Identify the exact order, guest, station, transaction, custody, and time period covered by curbside pickup.
2. Conduct the transfer directly whenever possible and use the approved written or electronic record.
3. Review active work, deadlines, high-risk conditions, and items awaiting manager action.
4. Confirm guest arrival, vehicle description or pickup location, guest name, order number, and payment status.
5. Carry the sealed order by the approved route while watching traffic, curbs, weather, and parking-lot hazards.
6. Verify the recipient before release and complete any permitted payment or identification requirement.
7. Provide receipt or final instructions and close the pickup in the ordering system.
8. Require repeat-back of critical allergy, cash, identity, safety, timing, and guest information.
9. Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
10. Both employees acknowledge the transfer; responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.

CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the correct guest, table, order, service trigger, and manager escalation point for curbside
pickup.
□ Confirm guest arrival, vehicle description or pickup location, guest name, order number, and payment status.
□ Carry the sealed order by the approved route while watching traffic, curbs, weather, and parking-lot hazards.
□ Verify the recipient before release and complete any permitted payment or identification requirement.
□ Provide receipt or final instructions and close the pickup in the ordering system.
□ Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
• Giving a general verbal update instead of exact identifiers and current status for curbside pickup.
• Failing to repeat back allergy, identity, cash, timing, or safety information.
• Assuming the receiver accepted responsibility without clear acknowledgment.
• Omitting an unresolved item, named owner, deadline, or manager notification.
• Discarding the source ticket, log, or record before the transfer is verified.

68. Delivery Driver Waiting Area Control SOP

SOP ID V1-068 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To keep delivery drivers in an approved waiting area that protects guest flow, order security, employee
access, and pickup sequence.
APPLIES TO: Assigned employees, shift leads, and managers responsible for the process.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee checks the current standard and communicates changes. The
manager authorizes exceptions and verifies correction.
WHEN USED: Whenever delivery driver waiting area control is required during opening, service, shift transition, or
closing.
• Order labels, packaging, and tamper-evident supplies
• Pickup, driver, or vehicle verification information
• Current status display, report, or checklist
• Approved timing, quality, capacity, or safety standard
• Employee and station assignments
• Status is checked against an approved timing, quality, safety, or capacity standard.
• Changes are communicated early enough for employees and guests to respond.
• Exceptions are recorded instead of relying on verbal memory.
• Management intervenes when the process exceeds authority, capacity, or safe limits.
1. Review current status, timing, staffing, volume, standards, and manager priorities relevant to delivery driver waiting area
control.
2. Observe the actual process and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify immediate hazards, delays, capacity limits, mismatches, and work without a clear owner.
4. Direct drivers to the designated waiting or pickup location and explain restricted areas.
5. Confirm the order being collected and provide an honest status without promising an unverified time.
6. Keep doors, aisles, host areas, emergency routes, and guest seating free from crowding.
7. Escalate aggressive behavior, unauthorized access, excessive delay, or platform disputes to the manager.
8. Assign corrections to named employees, communicate status, and set completion and recheck times.
9. Recheck the highest-risk items and escalate when the process exceeds authority, capacity, or safe limits.
10. Record decisions, exceptions, and follow-up for management review.


68. Delivery Driver Waiting Area Control SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the correct guest, table, order, service trigger, and manager escalation point for delivery
driver waiting area control.
□ Direct drivers to the designated waiting or pickup location and explain restricted areas.
□ Confirm the order being collected and provide an honest status without promising an unverified time.
□ Keep doors, aisles, host areas, emergency routes, and guest seating free from crowding.
□ Escalate aggressive behavior, unauthorized access, excessive delay, or platform disputes to the manager.
□ Recheck the highest-risk items and escalate when the process exceeds authority, capacity, or safe limits.
• Reviewing delivery driver waiting area control from a screen or report without checking the actual operation.
• Using an assumed target instead of the current approved timing, quality, capacity, or safety standard.
• Waiting until the problem affects guests or employees before communicating the change.
• Assigning a correction without a named owner, deadline, and recheck.
• Closing the review while an exception remains undocumented or beyond current authority.

69. To-Go Guest Complaint Handling SOP

SOP ID V1-069 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To resolve to-go complaints by confirming the order and facts, protecting safety, assigning authorized
recovery, and documenting follow-up.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service leaders, managers, and authorized follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee listens, confirms facts, and stays within authority. The manager selects recovery,
documents the decision, and assigns follow-up.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Order labels, packaging, and tamper-evident supplies
• Pickup, driver, or vehicle verification information
• Guest check, order, visit, and timing information
• Service-recovery authority and approval guide
• Manager communication
• The guest is heard without interruption and the concern is restated accurately.
• Safety, allergy, illness, injury, harassment, or threatening behavior is escalated immediately.
• Recovery stays within approved authority and is proportionate to the verified concern.
• The resolution, guest response, approval, and promised follow-up are documented.
1. Listen without interruption and identify the guest, order, visit timing, and exact concern related to handling the to-go complaint.
2. Acknowledge the concern without arguing, blaming, admitting liability, or promising an outcome outside authority.
3. Verify relevant ticket, product, employee, payment, timing, and prior-action facts while keeping the guest informed.
4. Record guest name, order number, contact information, channel, and the exact missing, incorrect, delayed, or quality concern.
5. Compare the complaint with the ticket, payment, packaging check, and employee information.
6. Apologize for the experience and obtain manager authority before promising refunds, credits, or replacements.
7. Confirm the approved resolution, timing, pickup or delivery plan, and documented follow-up.
8. Explain the approved resolution, responsible manager, expected timing, and any action the guest must take.
9. Follow up before departure or by the promised deadline and confirm whether another authorized response is needed.
10. Document the concern, facts reviewed, authority used, resolution, guest response, and assigned follow-up.


69. To-Go Guest Complaint Handling SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the correct guest, table, order, service trigger, and manager escalation point for to-go guest
complaint handling.
□ Record guest name, order number, contact information, channel, and the exact missing, incorrect, delayed, or quality
concern.
□ Compare the complaint with the ticket, payment, packaging check, and employee information.
□ Apologize for the experience and obtain manager authority before promising refunds, credits, or replacements.
□ Confirm the approved resolution, timing, pickup or delivery plan, and documented follow-up.
□ Follow up before departure or by the promised deadline and confirm whether another authorized response is needed.
• Offering recovery for to-go guest complaint handling before hearing the full concern and confirming the guest, order,
timing, and facts.
• Arguing, blaming another employee, minimizing the concern, or making an unsupported promise.
• Offering compensation outside authority or without recording the reason and approval.
• Failing to explain the resolution, expected timing, responsible manager, or remaining guest action.
• Ending the recovery without follow-up, guest-response notes, and pattern documentation.

70. To-Go Station Closing SOP

SOP ID V1-070 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To close the to-go station by reconciling active orders, cleaning and securing the area, restocking
supplies, and documenting unresolved issues.
APPLIES TO: Assigned closing employees, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Assigned employees complete closing work. The manager verifies sanitation, cash, security,
and unresolved conditions before release.
WHEN USED: After active service ends and before employees are released or the building is secured.
• Order labels, packaging, and tamper-evident supplies
• Pickup, driver, or vehicle verification information
• Current closing checklist
• Approved cleaning and sanitizing supplies
• Storage, waste, and security controls
• All guests, transactions, product, cash, equipment, and records are resolved or transferred.
• Cleaning, storage, waste, and security follow approved standards.
• Damage, shortages, incidents, and unfinished work are documented.
• The employee leaves only after management verifies the closing assignment.
1. Review the current to-go station closing checklist and confirm management has approved the start of closing work.
2. Identify active guests, orders, checks, cash, controlled product, and duties that must remain available.
3. Gather approved cleaning, storage, waste, and security supplies without obstructing operating areas.
4. Test or secure ordering systems, phones, tablets, printers, payment devices, and delivery platforms.
5. Stock or reconcile packaging, labels, bags, utensils, condiments, tamper-evident supplies, and receipts.
6. Clean and organize assembly, staging, pickup, curbside, and driver-waiting areas.
7. Review or close active orders, menu availability, allergy notes, shortages, waste, and unresolved guest issues.
8. Report breakage, shortage, maintenance, safety, cash, or guest issues and assign unresolved follow-up.
9. Complete the final checklist and walk the area with the manager or designated verifier.
10. Sign off only after work is accepted and remaining responsibility is formally transferred.

CUSTOMIZATION

closing.
□ Test or secure ordering systems, phones, tablets, printers, payment devices, and delivery platforms.

□ Review or close active orders, menu availability, allergy notes, shortages, waste, and unresolved guest issues.
□ Complete the final checklist and walk the area with the manager or designated verifier.
• Starting to-go station closing while guests, checks, orders, or required service functions are still active.
• Mixing cleaning, product storage, cash, waste, and security work without the approved sequence.
• Leaving shortages, damage, maintenance, or safety concerns undocumented.
• Releasing keys, cash, product, or records without confirmed custody.
• Leaving before the manager accepts the closing assignment.

PART 4

BAR & BEVERAGE OPERATIONS

25 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control bar readiness, beverage consistency, responsible alcohol service, cash and inventory,
equipment, cleaning, and shift accountability.


71. Bar Opening SOP

SOP ID V1-071 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To open the bar with clean equipment, verified cash and POS controls, stocked products, safe work
areas, and manager approval.
APPLIES TO: Assigned opening employees, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Assigned employees complete the opening work. The manager or shift lead verifies readiness
before service begins.
WHEN USED: Before the assigned station, department, or building begins service or changes opening
responsibility.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Current opening checklist
• Approved cleaning and sanitizing supplies
• Required stock and operating tools
• The area is clean, stocked, safe, and operational before service begins.
• Cash, product, keys, systems, and equipment are verified before use.
• Shortages, damage, hazards, and incomplete work are reported before guests are affected.
• Opening is not signed off until management verifies readiness.
1. Review the current bar opening checklist, expected business, assignments, and manager notes.
2. Gather approved tools and obtain only authorized cash, keys, product, or system access.
3. Inspect the assigned area for damage, contamination, shortages, unsafe conditions, and incomplete prior work.
4. Count or reconcile assigned cash, tabs, tips, comps, spills, controlled inventory, keys, and required records.
5. Inspect or secure refrigeration, draft systems, sinks, ice, glassware, tools, payment devices, and alcohol storage.
6. Prepare or store garnishes, mixers, juices, backups, and other perishables with approved dates, labels, and containers.
7. Review or document features, shortages, equipment problems, responsible-service concerns, cleaning, and manager sign-off.
8. Correct and report shortages, equipment problems, unsafe conditions, or incomplete work before service.
9. Complete a final readiness check and demonstrate critical controls requested by management.
10. Record completion and obtain manager or shift-lead release before the area begins service.

□ Review or document features, shortages, equipment problems, responsible-service concerns, cleaning, and manager

□ Complete a final readiness check and demonstrate critical controls requested by management.
• Starting bar opening from memory instead of the current checklist and manager notes.
• Opening with a shortage, hazard, unverified system, or incomplete prior-shift work.
• Using unassigned cash, keys, product, credentials, or equipment access.
• Correcting a problem without recording it or informing the responsible manager.
• Signing the area ready before the final manager verification.

73. Bar Guest Greeting SOP

SOP ID V1-073 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To acknowledge bar guests promptly, confirm seating and service needs, and establish who is
responsible for the guest experience.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever bar guest greeting is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for bar guest greeting.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Acknowledge the guest within the restaurant standard even while completing another transaction.
5. Provide menus or guidance and determine whether age verification is required before alcohol service.
6. Ask focused questions about beverage, food, seating, or timing needs without assuming alcohol consumption.
7. Enter the first order accurately and provide a realistic update when service is delayed.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

• Using an unsafe route, overloaded tray, unapproved handling method, or intrusive guest approach.
• Failing to communicate a delay, rejected item, missing need, or completed handoff promptly.
• Resetting the area or moving to another task while a guest need or exception remains unassigned.

75. Drink Preparation SOP

SOP ID V1-075 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prepare each beverage from the approved recipe using correct ingredients, portions, glassware,
garnish, sanitation, and presentation.
APPLIES TO: Trained bar, beverage, service, or preparation employees, plus trainers and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows approved recipes, tools, sanitation, and quality controls. The
manager verifies exceptions and waste.
WHEN USED: Whenever drink preparation is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Current approved recipe or process guide
• Clean tools, glassware, containers, or equipment
• Measured portioning or handling controls
• Only clean, approved tools, products, containers, recipes, and methods are used.
• Portion, labeling, storage, quality, and cross-contact standards are followed.
• Unsafe, damaged, spoiled, or questionable items are removed from use.
• Waste, shortage, malfunction, and recurring quality problems are reported.
1. Review the current drink preparation recipe, process, manufacturer instruction, assignment, or production need.
2. Prepare a clean, organized area with approved tools, containers, and protective equipment.
3. Inspect product, ingredients, glassware, containers, and equipment and remove unsafe or unapproved items.
4. Read the full ticket or approved recipe, including size, brand, modifier, allergy note, glass, portion, and garnish.
5. Measure controlled ingredients with the required jigger, pour device, or portion tool.
6. Build, shake, stir, blend, or pour in the approved sequence while preventing cross-contact and waste.
7. Inspect fill, appearance, garnish, glass, and ticket identity before placing the drink in the pickup position.
8. Check portion, quality, label, temperature, cleanliness, safety, and final presentation against standard.
9. Correct or discard unacceptable work and report recurring waste, malfunction, or shortage.
10. Clean, store, reset, and document the area or equipment before the task is complete.

□ Build, shake, stir, blend, or pour in the approved sequence while preventing cross-contact and waste.
□ Inspect fill, appearance, garnish, glass, and ticket identity before placing the drink in the pickup position.
□ Correct or discard unacceptable work and report recurring waste, malfunction, or shortage.
• Beginning drink preparation without the current recipe, approved tools, clean equipment, and correct product.
• Estimating portions, substitutions, temperatures, or preparation steps instead of following the standard.
• Using contaminated, damaged, expired, mislabeled, or unapproved ingredients, glassware, or equipment.
• Serving or storing work that fails quality, sanitation, portion, or presentation checks.
• Failing to record waste, shortage, malfunction, correction, and final reset.

77. Responsible Alcohol Service SOP

SOP ID V1-077 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To serve alcohol responsibly by monitoring guest condition, drink pace, food intake, age requirements,
and manager escalation triggers.
APPLIES TO: Bartenders, servers, hosts where applicable, security employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Every employee stops and escalates questionable alcohol service. The manager leads refusal,
safety planning, and documentation.
WHEN USED: Before and throughout any alcohol-related transaction or whenever eligibility or safety is uncertain.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Responsible alcohol-service policy
• Acceptable identification guide
• Guest check or service history
• Alcohol service follows applicable law, licensing requirements, training, and company policy.
• Age and identification are verified whenever required; employees do not guess.
• Service stops and management is notified when safety or eligibility is uncertain.
• Refusals and significant concerns are handled calmly and documented objectively.
1. Review the restaurant's current alcohol-service policy, state/local requirements, and manager escalation contacts before
service.
2. Confirm every alcohol-serving employee is trained and authorized for the assigned role.
3. Observe guest age, behavior, speech, coordination, pace of consumption, food intake, and group dynamics without
stereotyping.
4. Verify identification whenever required and use only approved forms of identification and inspection methods.
5. Serve only approved portions and recipes; do not free-pour, over-serve, encourage rapid consumption, or bypass refusal
decisions.
6. Communicate concerns discreetly with the manager and other authorized servers so service limits remain consistent.
7. Slow, delay, substitute, or stop service only within approved authority and offer nonalcoholic options or food when appropriate.
8. Arrange safe-transport assistance through the approved process without promising custody or transportation the restaurant
cannot provide.
9. Document refusals, incidents, disputed identification, threats, injuries, or law-enforcement contact according to policy.
10. Transfer unresolved concerns during handoff and confirm management ownership before leaving the area.


77. Responsible Alcohol Service SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can state the product or service trigger, responsible-service limit, assigned authority, and escalation
point for responsible alcohol service.
□ Confirm every alcohol-serving employee is trained and authorized for the assigned role.
□ Verify identification whenever required and use only approved forms of identification and inspection methods.
□ Communicate concerns discreetly with the manager and other authorized servers so service limits remain consistent.
□ Arrange safe-transport assistance through the approved process without promising custody or transportation the
restaurant cannot provide.
□ Transfer unresolved concerns during handoff and confirm management ownership before leaving the area.
• Treating responsible service as only an age-check issue and ignoring pace, behavior, impairment, and transportation
risk.
• Free-pouring, over-serving, encouraging rapid consumption, or allowing another employee to bypass a service limit.
• Waiting until a guest is visibly out of control before involving management.
• Arguing, shaming, stereotyping, or physically confronting a guest unnecessarily.
• Failing to document refusal, transportation, responder contact, and employee handoff.

78. Age Verification SOP

SOP ID V1-078 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify legal drinking age using approved identification standards before alcohol is served or sold.
APPLIES TO: Bartenders, servers, hosts where applicable, security employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Every employee stops and escalates questionable alcohol service. The manager leads refusal,
safety planning, and documentation.
WHEN USED: Before and throughout any alcohol-related transaction or whenever eligibility or safety is uncertain.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Responsible alcohol-service policy
• Acceptable identification guide
• Guest check or service history
• Alcohol service follows applicable law, licensing requirements, training, and company policy.
• Age and identification are verified whenever required; employees do not guess.
• Service stops and management is notified when safety or eligibility is uncertain.
• Refusals and significant concerns are handled calmly and documented objectively.
1. Identify every alcohol purchase or service situation that requires age verification under restaurant policy or law.
2. Ask the guest for an approved, unexpired identification document before preparing or delivering alcohol.
3. Hold the identification in the approved manner and compare the photograph, physical description, birth date, and expiration
date to the guest.
4. Check for alteration, damage, mismatched fonts, lifted photo, inconsistent laminate, or other signs of invalid identification.
5. Calculate age using the restaurant's approved method; do not rely on appearance, memory, or another employee's prior
check.
6. Ask only approved verification questions when identity remains uncertain and avoid retaining or photographing identification
unless authorized.
7. Decline service and notify the manager when identification is absent, expired, altered, borrowed, inconsistent, or otherwise
unacceptable.
8. Return the identification to the guest unless law or approved policy directs another action; do not confiscate it on personal
judgment.
9. Record disputed or refused identification when required and communicate the decision to authorized service employees.
10. Continue to monitor responsible service after age is verified; age verification does not remove intoxication controls.

CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can state the product or service trigger, responsible-service limit, assigned authority, and escalation

□ Ask the guest for an approved, unexpired identification document before preparing or delivering alcohol.

□ Ask only approved verification questions when identity remains uncertain and avoid retaining or photographing
identification unless authorized.
□ Return the identification to the guest unless law or approved policy directs another action; do not confiscate it on
personal judgment.
□ Continue to monitor responsible service after age is verified; age verification does not remove intoxication controls.
• Relying on appearance, familiarity, or another employee's prior check instead of inspecting identification.
• Accepting expired, altered, damaged, borrowed, photographed, or unapproved identification.
• Calculating age incorrectly or failing to compare the guest with the document.
• Confiscating or photographing identification outside law and approved policy.
• Treating age verification as permission to ignore later intoxication or conduct concerns.

79. Refusal of Alcohol Service SOP

SOP ID V1-079 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To refuse alcohol service calmly, consistently, and safely while involving management and
documenting the reason when required.
APPLIES TO: Bartenders, servers, hosts where applicable, security employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Every employee stops and escalates questionable alcohol service. The manager leads refusal,
safety planning, and documentation.
WHEN USED: Before and throughout any alcohol-related transaction or whenever eligibility or safety is uncertain.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Responsible alcohol-service policy
• Acceptable identification guide
• Guest check or service history
• Alcohol service follows applicable law, licensing requirements, training, and company policy.
• Age and identification are verified whenever required; employees do not guess.
• Service stops and management is notified when safety or eligibility is uncertain.
• Refusals and significant concerns are handled calmly and documented objectively.
1. Identify the reason service must be refused, including age, identification, intoxication, prior manager decision, behavior, or
policy restriction.
2. Notify the manager before refusal whenever time and safety allow and coordinate one consistent message.
3. Approach calmly, maintain safe distance, and use brief approved wording without accusation, debate, or public
embarrassment.
4. State that no additional alcohol will be served and do not negotiate another drink, another server, or a different payment
method.
5. Remove or secure pending alcohol only when safe and authorized; do not touch guest property or use physical force.
6. Offer approved nonalcoholic options, food, check settlement, or safe-transport assistance when appropriate.
7. Alert authorized employees so another server or bar position does not unknowingly continue service.
8. Escalate threats, violence, medical concerns, impaired driving risk, or refusal to leave according to the emergency and security
plan.
9. Document the time, reason, employees involved, guest response, manager action, and any transportation or responder
contact.
10. Review the incident at handoff and preserve relevant checks, video, witness, and transaction information.

CUSTOMIZATION

□ Notify the manager before refusal whenever time and safety allow and coordinate one consistent message.

□ Offer approved nonalcoholic options, food, check settlement, or safe-transport assistance when appropriate.
□ Escalate threats, violence, medical concerns, impaired driving risk, or refusal to leave according to the emergency and
security plan.
□ Review the incident at handoff and preserve relevant checks, video, witness, and transaction information.
• Refusing service without coordinating with the manager and other authorized servers.
• Debating, accusing, embarrassing, or threatening the guest.
• Allowing the guest to order through another employee after refusal.
• Touching the guest, their property, or their drink when it is not safe or authorized.
• Failing to document the reason, guest response, transportation, threats, and manager actions.

80. Intoxicated Guest Escalation SOP

SOP ID V1-080 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect an apparently intoxicated guest and others by stopping service, notifying management,
arranging safe next steps, and documenting the event.
APPLIES TO: Bartenders, servers, hosts where applicable, security employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Every employee stops and escalates questionable alcohol service. The manager leads refusal,
safety planning, and documentation.
WHEN USED: Before and throughout any alcohol-related transaction or whenever eligibility or safety is uncertain.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Responsible alcohol-service policy
• Acceptable identification guide
• Guest check or service history
• Alcohol service follows applicable law, licensing requirements, training, and company policy.
• Age and identification are verified whenever required; employees do not guess.
• Service stops and management is notified when safety or eligibility is uncertain.
• Refusals and significant concerns are handled calmly and documented objectively.
1. Observe and confirm specific behaviors that may indicate impairment, medical distress, escalating aggression, or inability to
travel safely.
2. Notify the manager immediately and stop additional alcohol service while the situation is assessed.
3. Review the guest's check, known consumption, food intake, companions, prior refusals, and transportation plan without
making a diagnosis.
4. Approach with a second authorized employee when appropriate and use calm, nonjudgmental language.
5. Remove additional alcohol only when safe and authorized; provide water, food, or a quieter area if approved and helpful.
6. Encourage a sober companion, rideshare, taxi, or other approved safe-transport option and record who accepted responsibility
when policy requires.
7. Do not allow employees to physically restrain, search, or transport the guest unless specifically trained and authorized.
8. Call emergency services or law enforcement when there is medical distress, violence, a weapon, immediate driving danger, or
another urgent threat.
9. Protect other guests, employees, exits, and evidence while maintaining privacy and avoiding unnecessary confrontation.
10. Complete the incident record and manager follow-up, including service history, decisions, transportation, responders, and
employee handoff.


80. Intoxicated Guest Escalation SOP - VERIFICATION
& CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can state the product or service trigger, responsible-service limit, assigned authority, and escalation
point for intoxicated guest escalation.
□ Notify the manager immediately and stop additional alcohol service while the situation is assessed.
□ Approach with a second authorized employee when appropriate and use calm, nonjudgmental language.
□ Encourage a sober companion, rideshare, taxi, or other approved safe-transport option and record who accepted
responsibility when policy requires.
□ Call emergency services or law enforcement when there is medical distress, violence, a weapon, immediate driving
danger, or another urgent threat.
□ Complete the incident record and manager follow-up, including service history, decisions, transportation, responders,
and employee handoff.
• Treating suspected impairment as a diagnosis or relying on one behavior without reviewing the situation.
• Continuing alcohol service while management decides what to do.
• Physically restraining, searching, or personally transporting the guest without training and authority.
• Ignoring medical distress, aggression, weapons, or immediate impaired-driving risk.
• Closing the incident without objective observations, service history, transportation, responder, and follow-up records.

81. Service Well SOP

SOP ID V1-081 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To operate the service well so drink tickets are prioritized, prepared accurately, staged safely, and
transferred to servers without delay or confusion.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever service well is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for service well.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Organize current tickets, clean tools, glassware, garnishes, ingredients, and designated pickup positions.
5. Follow the approved first-in sequence while recognizing allergy, remake, missing-drink, and manager-approved priorities.
6. Place completed drinks with clear ticket, table, and seat identification and communicate realistic delays.
7. Report unclaimed drinks, recurring waits, shortages, or quality decline before remaking or discarding.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

83. Draft Beer Service SOP

SOP ID V1-083 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To pour and serve draft beer with the correct glassware, temperature, head, portion, sanitation, and
product-loss controls.
APPLIES TO: Trained bar, beverage, service, or preparation employees, plus trainers and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows approved recipes, tools, sanitation, and quality controls. The
manager verifies exceptions and waste.
WHEN USED: Whenever draft beer service is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Current approved recipe or process guide
• Clean tools, glassware, containers, or equipment
• Measured portioning or handling controls
• Only clean, approved tools, products, containers, recipes, and methods are used.
• Portion, labeling, storage, quality, and cross-contact standards are followed.
• Unsafe, damaged, spoiled, or questionable items are removed from use.
• Waste, shortage, malfunction, and recurring quality problems are reported.
1. Confirm the correct beer, keg, tap, glassware, temperature, and service size before pouring.
2. Inspect the glass for cleanliness, chips, lipstick, detergent film, odor, and correct style.
3. Open the tap fully in one motion; do not partially open it or allow the faucet to contact the glass or beer.
4. Hold the glass at the approved angle and position to control foam without touching the faucet.
5. Straighten the glass near completion and create the restaurant's approved head.
6. Stop and report excessive foam, flat beer, off odor, unusual color, warm product, slow flow, or suspected line contamination.
7. Never mix old and new pours, return overflow to service, or serve from an unapproved tap.
8. Wipe the exterior, verify presentation, and deliver promptly with the correct check or ticket.
9. Record waste or comp according to policy and communicate keg or line concerns to the manager.
10. Keep the faucet, drip tray, surrounding area, and glass-storage zone clean during service.

• Serving excessive foam, flat beer, off odor, wrong product, or poor temperature without investigation.
• Mixing old and new pours or returning overflow to service.
• Failing to report line, keg, regulator, temperature, or sanitation problems.
• Omitting waste, comp, keg-change, or maintenance documentation.

glassware, temperature, and inventory control.
APPLIES TO: Trained bar, beverage, service, or preparation employees, plus trainers and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows approved recipes, tools, sanitation, and quality controls. The
manager verifies exceptions and waste.
WHEN USED: Whenever bottled and canned beverage service is required during opening, service, shift transition,
or closing.

• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Current approved recipe or process guide
• Clean tools, glassware, containers, or equipment

• Portion, labeling, storage, quality, and cross-contact standards are followed.
• Unsafe, damaged, spoiled, or questionable items are removed from use.
• Waste, shortage, malfunction, and recurring quality problems are reported.

4. Open the container using the approved tool and away from guests, food, and breakable items.
5. Do not place the opener, cap, bottle neck, or can rim on unclean surfaces.

7. Present or dispose of the cap, cork, or container according to policy and guest preference.

9. Record damaged product, wrong pulls, comps, or waste and return unopened product to the correct storage location.

85. Wine Service SOP

SOP ID V1-085 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To present, open, pour, and serve wine according to the restaurant's service level, responsible-service
policy, and product standards.
APPLIES TO: Trained bar, beverage, service, or preparation employees, plus trainers and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows approved recipes, tools, sanitation, and quality controls. The
manager verifies exceptions and waste.
WHEN USED: Whenever wine service is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Current approved recipe or process guide
• Clean tools, glassware, containers, or equipment
• Measured portioning or handling controls
• Only clean, approved tools, products, containers, recipes, and methods are used.
• Portion, labeling, storage, quality, and cross-contact standards are followed.
• Unsafe, damaged, spoiled, or questionable items are removed from use.
• Waste, shortage, malfunction, and recurring quality problems are reported.
1. Confirm the wine, vintage when relevant, bottle or glass size, price, service temperature, and guest order.
2. Select clean, odor-free, chip-free glassware appropriate to the restaurant's service standard.
3. Present the bottle label to the ordering guest before opening when full-bottle service is used.
4. Open the bottle safely with the approved tool and inspect the closure, neck, and wine for obvious defects.
5. Offer the approved verification pour when required and wait for guest confirmation without debating preference.
6. Pour the approved portion without touching the glass rim or overfilling.
7. Keep the label visible, wipe drips, and place or chill the bottle according to the service standard.
8. For by-the-glass service, protect open bottles with the approved date, preservation, storage, and discard controls.
9. Escalate suspected cork taint, oxidation, wrong vintage, broken cork, or guest rejection to the manager.
10. Record waste, replacement, comp, or open-bottle transfer and return tools and wine to proper storage.

CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can state the product or service trigger, responsible-service limit, assigned authority, and escalation
point for wine service.
□ Select clean, odor-free, chip-free glassware appropriate to the restaurant's service standard.
□ Open the bottle safely with the approved tool and inspect the closure, neck, and wine for obvious defects.
□ Pour the approved portion without touching the glass rim or overfilling.
□ For by-the-glass service, protect open bottles with the approved date, preservation, storage, and discard controls.
□ Record waste, replacement, comp, or open-bottle transfer and return tools and wine to proper storage.
• Opening the wrong wine, vintage, size, or price before guest confirmation.
• Using dirty, chipped, warm, or strongly scented glassware.
• Overpouring, touching the rim, hiding the label, or skipping the approved verification pour.
• Serving oxidized, corked, improperly stored, or overdue open wine.
• Failing to document replacement, waste, comp, and open-bottle control.

86. Cocktail Garnish Preparation SOP

SOP ID V1-086 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prepare, label, store, rotate, and handle cocktail garnishes in a sanitary way that protects quality,
allergens, and waste control.
APPLIES TO: Trained bar, beverage, service, or preparation employees, plus trainers and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows approved recipes, tools, sanitation, and quality controls. The
manager verifies exceptions and waste.
WHEN USED: Whenever cocktail garnish preparation is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Current approved recipe or process guide
• Clean tools, glassware, containers, or equipment
• Measured portioning or handling controls
• Only clean, approved tools, products, containers, recipes, and methods are used.
• Portion, labeling, storage, quality, and cross-contact standards are followed.
• Unsafe, damaged, spoiled, or questionable items are removed from use.
• Waste, shortage, malfunction, and recurring quality problems are reported.
1. Review the current cocktail garnish preparation recipe, process, manufacturer instruction, assignment, or production need.
2. Prepare a clean, organized area with approved tools, containers, and protective equipment.
3. Inspect product, ingredients, glassware, containers, and equipment and remove unsafe or unapproved items.
4. Clean and sanitize the prep area and gather approved produce, tools, containers, and labels.
5. Inspect ingredients and discard spoiled, damaged, contaminated, or unapproved product.
6. Cut and portion garnishes to the current recipe using safe knife and food-handling practices.
7. Date, label, cover, store, rotate, and replenish garnishes and record unusual waste.
8. Check portion, quality, label, temperature, cleanliness, safety, and final presentation against standard.
9. Correct or discard unacceptable work and report recurring waste, malfunction, or shortage.
10. Clean, store, reset, and document the area or equipment before the task is complete.

87. Bar Cash Handling SOP

SOP ID V1-087 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To receive, secure, process, and reconcile bar cash accurately while preserving employee
accountability and the transaction audit trail.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Identify the employee, guest check, drawer, payment, item, amount, and reason connected to bar cash handling.
2. Use only individual credentials and confirm the correct system, business date, location, and assigned custody.
3. Review the original ticket, receipt, authorization, balance, or supporting record before moving value.
4. Count and acknowledge the assigned bank before transactions and keep personal money, tips, and restaurant funds
separated.
5. Enter every sale before payment and place cash directly in the assigned drawer.
6. Provide correct change and complete drops, transfers, and paid-outs only through the approved process.
7. Reconcile sales, cash, tips, receipts, and variances with the manager before surrendering custody.
8. Stop for manager review when identity, amount, authority, payment data, cash count, or audit information does not agree.
9. Verify the final balance, receipt, tender, reason code, custody, and system status.
10. Secure funds and records and document approvals, variances, disputes, and unusual activity.


87. Bar Cash Handling SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can state the product or service trigger, responsible-service limit, assigned authority, and escalation
point for bar cash handling.
□ Count and acknowledge the assigned bank before transactions and keep personal money, tips, and restaurant funds
separated.
□ Enter every sale before payment and place cash directly in the assigned drawer.
□ Provide correct change and complete drops, transfers, and paid-outs only through the approved process.
□ Reconcile sales, cash, tips, receipts, and variances with the manager before surrendering custody.
□ Verify the final balance, receipt, tender, reason code, custody, and system status.
• Beginning bar cash handling without verifying the check, drawer, amount, reason, authority, and business date.
• Sharing credentials, bypassing required approval, or moving value outside the recorded system.
• Counting or reviewing funds where unauthorized employees or guests can observe or interfere.
• Forcing a transaction through when the receipt, tender, balance, or audit record does not agree.
• Closing the record without manager verification, secure custody, and documented variance.

90. Bar Glassware Handling SOP

SOP ID V1-090 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To wash, inspect, polish, handle, and store bar glassware without contaminating rims, nesting glasses,
or returning damaged pieces to service.
APPLIES TO: Trained bar, beverage, service, or preparation employees, plus trainers and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows approved recipes, tools, sanitation, and quality controls. The
manager verifies exceptions and waste.
WHEN USED: Whenever bar glassware handling is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide

• Current approved recipe or process guide

91. Bar Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V1-091 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To keep the bar clean and sanitary during service and complete the approved closing-cleaning
sequence without contaminating food or beverages.
APPLIES TO: Assigned closing employees, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Assigned employees complete closing work. The manager verifies sanitation, cash, security,
and unresolved conditions before release.
WHEN USED: Whenever bar cleaning is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Current beverage recipes and portion guide
• Responsible-service and age-verification resources
• Current closing checklist
• Approved cleaning and sanitizing supplies
• Storage, waste, and security controls
• All guests, transactions, product, cash, equipment, and records are resolved or transferred.
• Cleaning, storage, waste, and security follow approved standards.
• Damage, shortages, incidents, and unfinished work are documented.
• The employee leaves only after management verifies the closing assignment.
1. Review the current bar cleaning checklist and confirm management has approved the start of closing work.
2. Identify active guests, orders, checks, cash, controlled product, and duties that must remain available.
3. Gather approved cleaning, storage, waste, and security supplies without obstructing operating areas.
4. Remove food, glassware, tools, mats, trash, and movable supplies from the cleaning zone.
5. Wash, rinse, and sanitize food-contact tools and surfaces with approved chemical concentration and contact time.
6. Clean sinks, rails, wells, shelves, equipment exteriors, drains, floors, and high-touch points.
7. Air-dry and restore items and document maintenance, pest, chemical, or damage concerns.
8. Report breakage, shortage, maintenance, safety, cash, or guest issues and assign unresolved follow-up.
9. Complete the final checklist and walk the area with the manager or designated verifier.
10. Sign off only after work is accepted and remaining responsibility is formally transferred.

• Starting bar cleaning while guests, checks, orders, or required service functions are still active.
• Mixing cleaning, product storage, cash, waste, and security work without the approved sequence.
• Leaving shortages, damage, maintenance, or safety concerns undocumented.
• Releasing keys, cash, product, or records without confirmed custody.
• Leaving before the manager accepts the closing assignment.

94. Bar Shift Handoff SOP

SOP ID V1-094 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To transfer active guests, tabs, cash, product conditions, equipment issues, and unfinished bar duties
to the incoming employee.
APPLIES TO: Employees releasing or receiving the order, station, transaction, or responsibility, plus managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The releasing employee gives complete information. The receiving employee confirms
acceptance; management resolves exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever responsibility, custody, information, or an active order transfers between employees.
• Current order, guest, or work status
• Approved handoff record
• Identity, table, seat, or transaction information
• Manager communication
• Exception documentation
• The handoff is direct, complete, and acknowledged.
• Allergy, cash, guest, safety, identity, and timing information is repeated back when relevant.
• Responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.
• Every unresolved item has a named owner, deadline, and manager awareness.
1. Identify the exact order, guest, station, transaction, custody, and time period covered by bar shift handoff.
2. Conduct the transfer directly whenever possible and use the approved written or electronic record.
3. Review active work, deadlines, high-risk conditions, and items awaiting manager action.
4. Review active guests, tabs, pending food, drink tickets, refusals, and special requests face to face.
5. Count or transfer banks, keys, devices, controlled items, and signed receipts according to policy.
6. Identify shortages, draft or equipment issues, spills, prep levels, cleaning, and pending approvals.
7. Both employees confirm acceptance and management records unresolved financial, safety, or guest issues.
8. Require repeat-back of critical allergy, cash, identity, safety, timing, and guest information.
9. Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
10. Both employees acknowledge the transfer; responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.


94. Bar Shift Handoff SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can state the product or service trigger, responsible-service limit, assigned authority, and escalation
point for bar shift handoff.
□ Review active guests, tabs, pending food, drink tickets, refusals, and special requests face to face.
□ Count or transfer banks, keys, devices, controlled items, and signed receipts according to policy.
□ Identify shortages, draft or equipment issues, spills, prep levels, cleaning, and pending approvals.
□ Both employees confirm acceptance and management records unresolved financial, safety, or guest issues.
□ Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
• Giving a general verbal update instead of exact identifiers and current status for bar shift handoff.
• Failing to repeat back allergy, identity, cash, timing, or safety information.
• Assuming the receiver accepted responsibility without clear acknowledgment.
• Omitting an unresolved item, named owner, deadline, or manager notification.
• Discarding the source ticket, log, or record before the transfer is verified.

95. Bar Incident Documentation SOP

SOP ID V1-095 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To document bar incidents promptly with objective facts, protected records, required notifications, and
assigned management follow-up.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Current ticket, table, order, or guest information
• Approved trays, service tools, or supplies
• Table and seat-position standard
• Required accompaniments or replacement items
• Server, expo, or manager communication
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for bar incident documentation.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Secure immediate safety and identify the reporting manager, location, time, people, and witnesses.
5. Record objective observations and actions in chronological order.
6. Attach relevant checks, receipts, refusal records, photographs, property details, or video-reference information as permitted.
7. Sign and route the report confidentially without opinions, diagnosis, blame, or unauthorized promises.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.


95. Bar Incident Documentation SOP - VERIFICATION
& CUSTOMIZATION

□ Secure immediate safety and identify the reporting manager, location, time, people, and witnesses.

□ Attach relevant checks, receipts, refusal records, photographs, property details, or video-reference information as
permitted.
□ Sign and route the report confidentially without opinions, diagnosis, blame, or unauthorized promises.
□ Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
• Beginning bar incident documentation without confirming the guest, table, seat, item, timing, and responsible employee.
• Skipping an allergy, temperature, quality, identity, readiness, or safe-handling check to save time.
• Using an unsafe route, overloaded tray, unapproved handling method, or intrusive guest approach.
• Failing to communicate a delay, rejected item, missing need, or completed handoff promptly.
• Resetting the area or moving to another task while a guest need or exception remains unassigned.

PART 5

GUEST EXPERIENCE, RECOVERY &
SPECIAL SITUATIONS

20 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs guide complaint response, service recovery, injury and safety concerns, accessibility, special
recognition, and documented follow-up.


96. Guest Complaint Response SOP

SOP ID V1-096 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To receive and resolve guest complaints promptly by listening, confirming facts, protecting safety,
staying within authority, and documenting follow-up.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service leaders, managers, and authorized follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee listens, confirms facts, and stays within authority. The manager selects recovery,
documents the decision, and assigns follow-up.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Guest check, order, visit, and timing information
• Service-recovery authority and approval guide
• Manager communication
• Complaint or recovery documentation form
• Guest follow-up contact fields
• The guest is heard without interruption and the concern is restated accurately.
• Safety, allergy, illness, injury, harassment, or threatening behavior is escalated immediately.
• Recovery stays within approved authority and is proportionate to the verified concern.
• The resolution, guest response, approval, and promised follow-up are documented.
1. Stop other tasks, face the guest, and listen without interruption.
2. Confirm the guest, table, check, timing, item, employee, and exact concern in the guest's own terms.
3. Acknowledge the impact without arguing, blaming, admitting liability, or promising an unauthorized outcome.
4. Determine whether the concern involves immediate safety, allergy, illness, injury, harassment, threats, payment, or property
loss.
5. Correct what is within the employee's approved authority and notify the manager for anything beyond it.
6. Keep the guest informed while facts, replacement timing, or recovery options are verified.
7. Confirm the agreed next step, responsible employee, expected timing, and any manager visit.
8. Check back after the correction and ask whether the immediate concern has been resolved.
9. Record required facts, recovery, product, payment, employee, and follow-up without labels or speculation.
10. Escalate repeated patterns, unresolved dissatisfaction, public-review threats, or safety concerns to management.


96. Guest Complaint Response SOP - VERIFICATION
& CUSTOMIZATION

□ Confirm the guest, table, check, timing, item, employee, and exact concern in the guest's own terms.
□ Determine whether the concern involves immediate safety, allergy, illness, injury, harassment, threats, payment, or
property loss.
□ Keep the guest informed while facts, replacement timing, or recovery options are verified.
□ Check back after the correction and ask whether the immediate concern has been resolved.
□ Escalate repeated patterns, unresolved dissatisfaction, public-review threats, or safety concerns to management.
• Offering recovery before fully hearing and confirming the concern.
• Arguing, blaming, minimizing, admitting liability, or promising an unauthorized outcome.
• Missing a safety, allergy, illness, injury, harassment, payment, or property issue that requires immediate escalation.
• Leaving the guest without a clear correction owner, timing, update, or check-back.
• Failing to document the complaint, recovery, guest response, and repeated pattern.

97. Manager Guest Complaint SOP

SOP ID V1-097 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To guide a manager-led response to a guest complaint, select proportionate recovery, verify
completion, and record unresolved or recurring issues.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service leaders, managers, and authorized follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee listens, confirms facts, and stays within authority. The manager selects recovery,
documents the decision, and assigns follow-up.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Guest check, order, visit, and timing information
• Service-recovery authority and approval guide
• Manager communication
• Complaint or recovery documentation form
• Guest follow-up contact fields
• The guest is heard without interruption and the concern is restated accurately.
• Safety, allergy, illness, injury, harassment, or threatening behavior is escalated immediately.
• Recovery stays within approved authority and is proportionate to the verified concern.
• The resolution, guest response, approval, and promised follow-up are documented.
1. Review the employee handoff, guest check, order, timing, prior actions, and any safety or legal concerns before approaching.
2. Introduce yourself, move the conversation to an appropriate location when possible, and invite the guest to explain.
3. Listen fully and restate the concern, desired outcome, and confirmed facts without defending the restaurant prematurely.
4. Separate service, product, timing, employee-conduct, payment, injury, illness, allergy, and policy issues.
5. Protect evidence and notify ownership, HR, insurance, security, or authorities when the issue requires specialized handling.
6. Select a proportionate recovery within authority and explain what can be done now, what needs review, and what cannot be
promised.
7. Assign the correction, timing, and responsible employee and personally verify the result.
8. Confirm the guest's preferred contact method and follow-up commitment when additional review is required.
9. Complete objective documentation, including statements, transactions, product, witnesses, recovery, and retained records.
10. Review the underlying system failure and assign coaching, repair, menu, staffing, or SOP follow-up.

CUSTOMIZATION

cannot be promised.
□ Confirm the guest's preferred contact method and follow-up commitment when additional review is required.
□ Review the underlying system failure and assign coaching, repair, menu, staffing, or SOP follow-up.
• Approaching without reviewing prior actions, facts, authority, and any safety or legal concern.
• Defending the restaurant before listening and restating the guest's concern.
• Combining service recovery with injury, illness, harassment, payment, or evidence handling that requires specialized
routing.
• Assigning a correction without personally verifying completion and guest follow-up.
• Closing the case without objective records and system-level corrective action.

98. Service Recovery Authorization SOP

SOP ID V1-098 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To define who may authorize service recovery, what limits apply, and how every approval, guest
response, and follow-up is documented.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service leaders, managers, and authorized follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee listens, confirms facts, and stays within authority. The manager selects recovery,
documents the decision, and assigns follow-up.
WHEN USED: Whenever service recovery authorization is required during opening, service, shift transition, or
closing.
• Guest check, order, visit, and timing information
• Service-recovery authority and approval guide
• Manager communication
• Complaint or recovery documentation form
• Guest follow-up contact fields
• The guest is heard without interruption and the concern is restated accurately.
• Safety, allergy, illness, injury, harassment, or threatening behavior is escalated immediately.
• Recovery stays within approved authority and is proportionate to the verified concern.
• The resolution, guest response, approval, and promised follow-up are documented.
1. Listen without interruption and identify the guest, order, visit timing, and exact concern connected to service recovery
authorization.
2. Acknowledge the concern without arguing, blaming, admitting liability, or promising an outcome outside authority.
3. Verify relevant ticket, product, employee, payment, timing, and prior-action facts while keeping the guest informed.
4. Identify the guest concern, affected item, check amount, prior actions, and requested resolution.
5. Confirm the employee and manager authority levels before committing to a remake, comp, discount, refund, or credit.
6. Enter the approved recovery under the correct POS reason code with required notes and credentials.
7. Communicate the action and timing to the guest and team and verify completion and pattern review.
8. Explain the approved resolution, responsible manager, expected timing, and any action the guest must take.
9. Follow up before departure or by the promised deadline and confirm whether another authorized response is needed.
10. Document the concern, facts reviewed, authority used, resolution, guest response, and assigned follow-up.

□ Follow up before departure or by the promised deadline and confirm whether another authorized response is needed.
• Offering recovery for service recovery authorization before hearing the full concern and confirming the guest, order,
timing, and facts.
• Arguing, blaming another employee, minimizing the concern, or making an unsupported promise.
• Offering compensation outside authority or without recording the reason and approval.
• Failing to explain the resolution, expected timing, responsible manager, or remaining guest action.
• Ending the recovery without follow-up, guest-response notes, and pattern documentation.

99. Incorrect Order Recovery SOP

SOP ID V1-099 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To correct an inaccurate or incomplete order quickly while protecting allergy controls, remake timing,
guest communication, and transaction accuracy.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service leaders, managers, and authorized follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee listens, confirms facts, and stays within authority. The manager selects recovery,
documents the decision, and assigns follow-up.
WHEN USED: Whenever incorrect order recovery is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Guest check, order, visit, and timing information
• Service-recovery authority and approval guide
• Manager communication
• Complaint or recovery documentation form
• Guest follow-up contact fields
• The guest is heard without interruption and the concern is restated accurately.
• Safety, allergy, illness, injury, harassment, or threatening behavior is escalated immediately.
• Recovery stays within approved authority and is proportionate to the verified concern.
• The resolution, guest response, approval, and promised follow-up are documented.
1. Listen without interruption and identify the guest, order, visit timing, and exact concern connected to incorrect order recovery.
2. Acknowledge the concern without arguing, blaming, admitting liability, or promising an outcome outside authority.
3. Verify relevant ticket, product, employee, payment, timing, and prior-action facts while keeping the guest informed.
4. Remove or isolate the incorrect item and confirm exactly what the guest originally requested.
5. Compare the guest statement, POS entry, kitchen ticket, and delivered item to identify the failure point.
6. Enter the correction or remake with priority, modifier, and allergy information and notify kitchen, expo, and manager.
7. Update the guest honestly, adjust the check as authorized, and follow up after replacement.
8. Explain the approved resolution, responsible manager, expected timing, and any action the guest must take.
9. Follow up before departure or by the promised deadline and confirm whether another authorized response is needed.
10. Document the concern, facts reviewed, authority used, resolution, guest response, and assigned follow-up.

102. Allergy Concern Response SOP

SOP ID V1-102 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To respond immediately to a guest-reported allergy concern, stop unsafe service, involve management,
preserve facts, and coordinate approved follow-up.
APPLIES TO: All guest-facing employees, shift leads, managers, and designated emergency or follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect immediate safety and notify management. The manager controls response,
recovery, records, and authorized follow-up.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Approved allergen and ingredient resources
• Allergy alert and manager escalation method
• Emergency and manager contacts
• Approved complaint or incident form
• Guest, employee, order, or transaction information
• Immediate safety and operational control come before recovery or paperwork.
• Employees do not diagnose, admit liability, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
• The scene, product, transaction, and witness information are preserved when required.
• Objective documentation and authorized follow-up are completed promptly.
1. Protect immediate safety and notify the manager before beginning the formal allergy concern response.
2. Contact emergency, security, law-enforcement, HR, ownership, or other approved resources when required.
3. Preserve the scene, item, check, records, and other evidence without creating additional risk.
4. Stop service of the questioned item and notify the manager and designated kitchen leader immediately.
5. Confirm what the guest reported without guessing ingredients, severity, diagnosis, or medical outcome.
6. Follow the emergency plan and contact emergency services when symptoms or an emergency are reported.
7. Preserve relevant ticket and food information and document objective facts for authorized follow-up.
8. Collect separate witness names and distinguish direct observations from information reported by others.
9. Do not diagnose, admit liability, assign unsupported blame, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
10. Complete, sign, secure, and route the report promptly and assign authorized follow-up.


102. Allergy Concern Response SOP - VERIFICATION
& CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the guest concern, immediate safety trigger, recovery authority, evidence needs, and
escalation point for allergy concern response.
□ Stop service of the questioned item and notify the manager and designated kitchen leader immediately.
□ Confirm what the guest reported without guessing ingredients, severity, diagnosis, or medical outcome.
□ Follow the emergency plan and contact emergency services when symptoms or an emergency are reported.
□ Preserve relevant ticket and food information and document objective facts for authorized follow-up.
□ Do not diagnose, admit liability, assign unsupported blame, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
• Delaying immediate safety action while gathering details or discussing recovery for allergy concern response.
• Diagnosing, admitting liability, assigning blame, or promising an outcome outside authority.
• Combining witness accounts or failing to preserve the item, scene, check, or records.
• Discussing private guest or employee information with uninvolved people.
• Failing to complete, secure, route, and assign follow-up for the incident record.

• Approved complaint or incident form

• Immediate safety and operational control come before recovery or paperwork.
• Employees do not diagnose, admit liability, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
• The scene, product, transaction, and witness information are preserved when required.
• Objective documentation and authorized follow-up are completed promptly.
1. Protect immediate safety and notify the manager before beginning the formal foreign object complaint response.
2. Contact emergency, security, law-enforcement, HR, ownership, or other approved resources when required.
3. Preserve the scene, item, check, records, and other evidence without creating additional risk.
4. Ask the guest to stop consuming the item and notify the manager and kitchen leader.
5. Preserve the object and food in a secure labeled container and prevent further service from a suspected source.
6. Record the item, table, ticket, time, object description, witnesses, and guest statement without speculating.
7. Provide approved recovery and medical assistance when needed and route evidence for investigation.
8. Collect separate witness names and distinguish direct observations from information reported by others.
9. Do not diagnose, admit liability, assign unsupported blame, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
10. Complete, sign, secure, and route the report promptly and assign authorized follow-up.

105. Guest Injury Initial Response SOP

SOP ID V1-105 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To provide an immediate, calm response to a guest injury, obtain appropriate help, secure the scene,
and document objective facts.
APPLIES TO: All guest-facing employees, shift leads, managers, and designated emergency or follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect immediate safety and notify management. The manager controls response,
recovery, records, and authorized follow-up.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Emergency, security, or medical contact information
• Incident, witness, and evidence-preservation forms
• Emergency and manager contacts
• Approved complaint or incident form
• Guest, employee, order, or transaction information
• Immediate safety and operational control come before recovery or paperwork.
• Employees do not diagnose, admit liability, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
• The scene, product, transaction, and witness information are preserved when required.
• Objective documentation and authorized follow-up are completed promptly.
1. Protect immediate safety and notify the manager before beginning the formal guest injury response.
2. Contact emergency, security, law-enforcement, HR, ownership, or other approved resources when required.
3. Preserve the scene, item, check, records, and other evidence without creating additional risk.
4. Stop nearby activity, protect the guest from additional danger, and contact the manager.
5. Ask whether emergency medical assistance is needed and call emergency services according to the response plan.
6. Do not move the guest unless immediate safety requires it and do not diagnose, assign fault, or offer medication.
7. Preserve the scene, identify witnesses, document objective facts, and route follow-up through management.
8. Collect separate witness names and distinguish direct observations from information reported by others.
9. Do not diagnose, admit liability, assign unsupported blame, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
10. Complete, sign, secure, and route the report promptly and assign authorized follow-up.

106. Disruptive Guest Response SOP

SOP ID V1-106 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To address disruptive guest behavior early while protecting safety, setting clear limits, involving
management, and documenting material events.
APPLIES TO: All guest-facing employees, shift leads, managers, and designated emergency or follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect immediate safety and notify management. The manager controls response,
recovery, records, and authorized follow-up.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Emergency, security, or medical contact information
• Incident, witness, and evidence-preservation forms
• Emergency and manager contacts
• Approved complaint or incident form
• Guest, employee, order, or transaction information

• Employees do not diagnose, admit liability, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
• The scene, product, transaction, and witness information are preserved when required.
• Objective documentation and authorized follow-up are completed promptly.
