107. Harassment or Threatening Behavior Escalation
SOP

SOP ID V1-107 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To escalate harassment or threatening behavior immediately, separate affected people when safe,
contact authorities when required, and preserve records.
APPLIES TO: All guest-facing employees, shift leads, managers, and designated emergency or follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect immediate safety and notify management. The manager controls response,
recovery, records, and authorized follow-up.
WHEN USED: Whenever harassment or threatening behavior escalation is required during opening, service, shift
transition, or closing.
• Emergency, security, or medical contact information
• Incident, witness, and evidence-preservation forms
• Emergency and manager contacts
• Approved complaint or incident form
• Guest, employee, order, or transaction information
• Immediate safety and operational control come before recovery or paperwork.
• Employees do not diagnose, admit liability, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
• The scene, product, transaction, and witness information are preserved when required.
• Objective documentation and authorized follow-up are completed promptly.
1. Protect immediate safety and notify the manager before beginning the formal harassment or threatening behavior escalation
response.
2. Contact emergency, security, law-enforcement, HR, ownership, or other approved resources when required.
3. Preserve the scene, item, check, records, and other evidence without creating additional risk.
4. Move the affected person to a safer location when possible and notify the manager immediately.
5. Separate parties without physical intervention unless trained and required for immediate safety.
6. Contact security, ownership, human resources, or law enforcement according to severity and policy.
7. Record exact words or actions, witnesses, timing, response, privacy controls, and nonretaliation expectations.
8. Collect separate witness names and distinguish direct observations from information reported by others.
9. Do not diagnose, admit liability, assign unsupported blame, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
10. Complete, sign, secure, and route the report promptly and assign authorized follow-up.

SOP ID V1-108 VERSION 1.0

concern is observed.
APPLIES TO: All guest-facing employees, shift leads, managers, and designated emergency or follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect immediate safety and notify management. The manager controls response,
recovery, records, and authorized follow-up.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.

109. Lost Child Response SOP

SOP ID V1-109 VERSION 1.0

separated, or found alone.
APPLIES TO: All guest-facing employees, shift leads, managers, and designated emergency or follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect immediate safety and notify management. The manager controls response,
recovery, records, and authorized follow-up.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.

110. Lost Property Return SOP

SOP ID V1-110 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To return lost property only after identity is reasonably verified, custody is documented, and the
authorized release process is completed.
APPLIES TO: Employees releasing or receiving the order, station, transaction, or responsibility, plus managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The releasing employee gives complete information. The receiving employee confirms
acceptance; management resolves exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever lost property return is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Lost-and-found custody log
• Property identification label or secure bag
• Guest identity and property-description information
• Manager communication
• Property return receipt
• The handoff is direct, complete, and acknowledged.
• Allergy, cash, guest, safety, identity, and timing information is repeated back when relevant.
• Responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.
• Every unresolved item has a named owner, deadline, and manager awareness.
1. Identify the exact order, guest, station, transaction, custody, and time period covered by lost property return.
2. Conduct the transfer directly whenever possible and use the approved written or electronic record.
3. Review active work, deadlines, high-risk conditions, and items awaiting manager action.
4. Ask the claimant to describe the item, identifying marks, contents, loss location, and approximate time before showing it.
5. Compare the description to the intake record and stored item while protecting private contents.
6. Obtain manager approval for high-value, identity, payment, key, electronic, or sensitive property.
7. Record claimant verification, date, time, employee, signature when required, and final disposition.
8. Require repeat-back of critical allergy, cash, identity, safety, timing, and guest information.
9. Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
10. Both employees acknowledge the transfer; responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.

□ Record claimant verification, date, time, employee, signature when required, and final disposition.
□ Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
• Giving a general verbal update instead of exact identifiers and current status for lost property return.
• Failing to repeat back allergy, identity, cash, timing, or safety information.
• Assuming the receiver accepted responsibility without clear acknowledgment.
• Omitting an unresolved item, named owner, deadline, or manager notification.
• Discarding the source ticket, log, or record before the transfer is verified.

111. Special Occasion Recognition SOP

SOP ID V1-111 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To recognize birthdays, anniversaries, celebrations, and other special occasions accurately without
making unauthorized promises or disrupting service.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever special occasion recognition is required during opening, service, shift transition, or
closing.
• Reservation and guest-profile information
• Approved special-occasion recognition options
• Menu, allergy, and timing information
• Manager authorization guide
• Recognition and follow-up notes
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for special occasion recognition.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Confirm the occasion, guest name, table, timing, privacy preference, and any purchased or complimentary item.
5. Review allergy, dietary, candle, singing, photography, and outside-item rules.
6. Coordinate timing with server, kitchen, bar, host, and manager so recognition fits the meal pace.
7. Deliver the approved recognition warmly and update the check and guest notes correctly.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

□ The employee can identify the guest concern, immediate safety trigger, recovery authority, evidence needs, and

□ Confirm the occasion, guest name, table, timing, privacy preference, and any purchased or complimentary item.
□ Review allergy, dietary, candle, singing, photography, and outside-item rules.
□ Coordinate timing with server, kitchen, bar, host, and manager so recognition fits the meal pace.
□ Deliver the approved recognition warmly and update the check and guest notes correctly.
□ Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
• Beginning special occasion recognition without confirming the guest, table, seat, item, timing, and responsible
employee.
• Skipping an allergy, temperature, quality, identity, readiness, or safe-handling check to save time.
• Using an unsafe route, overloaded tray, unapproved handling method, or intrusive guest approach.
• Failing to communicate a delay, rejected item, missing need, or completed handoff promptly.
• Resetting the area or moving to another task while a guest need or exception remains unassigned.

112. VIP and Regular Guest Recognition SOP

SOP ID V1-112 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To recognize VIPs and regular guests consistently while protecting privacy, honoring approved
preferences, and avoiding unfair or unrecorded exceptions.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever vip and regular guest recognition is required during opening, service, shift transition, or
closing.
• Approved guest-preference notes
• Reservation, POS, and visit-history information
• Privacy and data-use standard
• Manager recognition plan
• Guest follow-up fields
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for VIP and regular guest recognition.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Confirm identity through approved reservation or guest-profile information rather than assumptions.
5. Review documented preferences, restrictions, prior concerns, and authorized recognition.
6. Notify the manager and assigned server discreetly while maintaining normal standards for surrounding guests.
7. Record only appropriate service preferences and do not disclose spending, visit, or personal information.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

• Beginning VIP and regular guest recognition without confirming the guest, table, seat, item, timing, and responsible
employee.

• Failing to communicate a delay, rejected item, missing need, or completed handoff promptly.
• Resetting the area or moving to another task while a guest need or exception remains unassigned.

113. Accessibility Accommodation Request SOP

SOP ID V1-113 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To receive accessibility accommodation requests respectfully, identify practical options, avoid
assumptions, and route decisions requiring management review.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever accessibility accommodation request is required during opening, service, shift transition,
or closing.
• Guest accessibility request information
• Accessible seating, route, and facility options
• Approved accessibility equipment or aids
• Manager communication
• Accommodation request documentation
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for accessibility accommodation request.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Listen to the requested assistance and ask only what is necessary to understand the service need.
5. Do not challenge, diagnose, or assume; involve management when safety, policy, or available space is affected.
6. Offer approved options that preserve dignity and access while keeping routes and exits clear.
7. Confirm the solution with the guest and communicate only necessary operational information to the team.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

114. Language Barrier Assistance SOP

SOP ID V1-114 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To assist guests facing a language barrier through approved communication tools, respectful
confirmation, and manager support when needed.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service assistants, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee performs the service and communicates needs. The manager handles
exceptions and actions beyond employee authority.
WHEN USED: Whenever language barrier assistance is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Approved language-assistance resources
• Current menu, pricing, and allergen information
• Written, visual, or translation aids
• Manager communication
• Guest confirmation method
• The correct guest, table, seat, item, and order are confirmed before service.
• Food, beverages, serviceware, and guest property are handled safely.
• Allergy, temperature, quality, identity, and missing-item concerns are stopped and escalated.
• Completion and unresolved needs are communicated to the responsible employee or manager.
1. Confirm the correct guest, table, seat, order, timing, and responsible employee for language barrier assistance.
2. Inspect the ticket, item, tools, identifiers, and required accompaniments before beginning.
3. Choose the approved service method and safe route and obtain help rather than taking an unsafe shortcut.
4. Use simple respectful language, visual menu references, written numbers, approved translation tools, or a qualified employee
interpreter.
5. Confirm meaning through teach-back, pointing, or written confirmation rather than repeated louder speech.
6. Do not use children or unapproved bystanders for sensitive payment, medical, employment, or complaint interpretation.
7. Escalate unresolved allergy, safety, consent, or payment questions before proceeding.
8. Stop when identity, allergy, quality, temperature, or safety information does not match.
9. Notify the responsible employee or manager of completion and every remaining guest need.
10. Return equipment, reset the area or system, and document material exceptions before the next task.

115. Guest Feedback Documentation and Follow-Up
SOP

SOP ID V1-115 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To record guest feedback accurately, identify patterns, assign follow-up, and close the loop with the
guest and responsible manager when appropriate.
APPLIES TO: Guest-facing employees, service leaders, managers, and authorized follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee listens, confirms facts, and stays within authority. The manager selects recovery,
documents the decision, and assigns follow-up.
WHEN USED: Whenever guest feedback documentation and follow-up is required during opening, service, shift
transition, or closing.
• Guest feedback and contact information
• Related check, order, visit, and employee details
• Feedback classification and routing guide
• Follow-up owner and due-date fields
• Secure feedback documentation form
• The guest is heard without interruption and the concern is restated accurately.
• Safety, allergy, illness, injury, harassment, or threatening behavior is escalated immediately.
• Recovery stays within approved authority and is proportionate to the verified concern.
• The resolution, guest response, approval, and promised follow-up are documented.
1. Listen without interruption and identify the guest, order, visit timing, and exact concern connected to guest feedback
documentation and follow-up.
2. Acknowledge the concern without arguing, blaming, admitting liability, or promising an outcome outside authority.
3. Verify relevant ticket, product, employee, payment, timing, and prior-action facts while keeping the guest informed.
4. Record guest contact only with permission, plus visit details and the specific feedback.
5. Classify the issue and attach relevant check, order, employee, timing, or location information.
6. Assign a follow-up owner, due date, authority, and promised response while protecting guest information.
7. Complete the contact, document the outcome, and escalate unresolved or repeated patterns.
8. Explain the approved resolution, responsible manager, expected timing, and any action the guest must take.
9. Follow up before departure or by the promised deadline and confirm whether another authorized response is needed.
10. Document the concern, facts reviewed, authority used, resolution, guest response, and assigned follow-up.


115. Guest Feedback Documentation and Follow-Up
SOP - VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the guest concern, immediate safety trigger, recovery authority, evidence needs, and
escalation point for guest feedback documentation and follow-up.
□ Record guest contact only with permission, plus visit details and the specific feedback.
□ Classify the issue and attach relevant check, order, employee, timing, or location information.
□ Assign a follow-up owner, due date, authority, and promised response while protecting guest information.
□ Complete the contact, document the outcome, and escalate unresolved or repeated patterns.
□ Follow up before departure or by the promised deadline and confirm whether another authorized response is needed.
• Offering recovery for guest feedback documentation and follow-up before hearing the full concern and confirming the
guest, order, timing, and facts.
• Arguing, blaming another employee, minimizing the concern, or making an unsupported promise.
• Offering compensation outside authority or without recording the reason and approval.
• Failing to explain the resolution, expected timing, responsible manager, or remaining guest action.
• Ending the recovery without follow-up, guest-response notes, and pattern documentation.

POS, CASH, DISCOUNTS & FINANCIAL
CONTROLS

20 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs protect transaction accuracy, payment security, cash custody, approvals, discounts, refunds, gift
cards, and daily reconciliation.


116. POS Login and Security SOP

SOP ID V1-116 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect POS access and transaction accountability by requiring individual credentials, secure login
practices, role-based permissions, and prompt reporting of suspicious activity.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Identify the employee, guest check, drawer, payment, item, amount, and reason connected to POS login and security.
2. Use only individual credentials and confirm the correct system, business date, location, and assigned custody.
3. Review the original ticket, receipt, authorization, balance, or supporting record before moving value.
4. Use only individually assigned credentials and confirm the correct employee profile, location, drawer, printer, and permissions.
5. Lock or log out whenever leaving the device and shield credentials and guest information from view.
6. Never share passwords, cards, codes, or another employee profile to bypass permissions.
7. Report lost credentials, suspicious access, incorrect permissions, or compromised devices immediately.
8. Stop for manager review when identity, amount, authority, payment data, cash count, or audit information does not agree.
9. Verify the final balance, receipt, tender, reason code, custody, and system status.
10. Secure funds and records and document approvals, variances, disputes, and unusual activity.


116. POS Login and Security SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the authorized user, transaction record, approval requirement, custody point, and exception
trigger for POS login and security.
□ Use only individually assigned credentials and confirm the correct employee profile, location, drawer, printer, and
permissions.
□ Lock or log out whenever leaving the device and shield credentials and guest information from view.
□ Never share passwords, cards, codes, or another employee profile to bypass permissions.
□ Report lost credentials, suspicious access, incorrect permissions, or compromised devices immediately.
□ Verify the final balance, receipt, tender, reason code, custody, and system status.
• Beginning POS login and security without verifying the check, drawer, amount, reason, authority, and business date.
• Sharing credentials, bypassing required approval, or moving value outside the recorded system.
• Counting or reviewing funds where unauthorized employees or guests can observe or interfere.
• Forcing a transaction through when the receipt, tender, balance, or audit record does not agree.
• Closing the record without manager verification, secure custody, and documented variance.

SOP ID V1-117 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To enter every order in the POS accurately with the correct table, seat, course, item, quantity, price, tax,
and service instructions.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.

119. POS Table Transfer SOP

SOP ID V1-119 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To transfer a POS table only after verifying the correct checks, destination table, server responsibility,
guest status, and manager authority.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Confirm the guest is physically moving to a different table and identify the current table, destination table, server assignment,
and open checks involved.
2. Obtain manager approval when required, especially when the move crosses sections, combines parties, affects reservations,
or changes server ownership.
3. Verify that the destination table is available, clean, correctly configured, and not reserved or blocked in the POS or seating
system.
4. Review all open items, seat numbers, coursing notes, discounts, payments, and special instructions before beginning the
transfer.
5. Use the POS table-transfer function rather than reopening, duplicating, or manually recreating items.
6. Transfer the full table or approved seats to the exact destination and preserve guest counts, seat positions, modifiers, timing,
and check history.
7. Confirm the original table is cleared in the POS and the destination now displays every expected item and payment status.
8. Notify the host, receiving server, expo, bar, and manager when the move changes seating, service ownership, delivery
location, or section counts.
9. Correct any duplicate, missing, or misrouted item immediately and document manager intervention when financial records
were affected.
10. Reconfirm the guests' location and responsible server before leaving the transaction.

CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the authorized user, transaction record, approval requirement, custody point, and exception

□ Obtain manager approval when required, especially when the move crosses sections, combines parties, affects

□ Review all open items, seat numbers, coursing notes, discounts, payments, and special instructions before beginning
the transfer.
□ Transfer the full table or approved seats to the exact destination and preserve guest counts, seat positions, modifiers,
timing, and check history.
□ Notify the host, receiving server, expo, bar, and manager when the move changes seating, service ownership, delivery
location, or section counts.
□ Reconfirm the guests' location and responsible server before leaving the transaction.
• Moving guests before confirming that the destination table is available and approved.
• Recreating items manually instead of using the table-transfer function.
• Losing seat numbers, modifiers, payments, coursing notes, or transaction history.
• Failing to notify host, server, expo, or bar teams that the delivery location changed.
• Leaving items on both tables or clearing the original table before verifying the destination.

120. POS Check Transfer Between Servers SOP

SOP ID V1-120 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To transfer an open check between servers without losing payment custody, table responsibility,
discounts, tips, or the transaction audit trail.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Identify the specific guest check or seat being reassigned and confirm the releasing server, receiving server, table, and reason
for transfer.
2. Obtain required approval before transferring ownership, particularly after payment activity, discounts, alcohol service, tip entry,
or shift checkout has begun.
3. Have both servers review the open items, modifiers, coursing, allergies, payments, guest requests, and unresolved service
issues.
4. Select the approved POS check-transfer or server-transfer function; do not share login credentials or ring future items under
the wrong employee.
5. Transfer only the intended check, seats, or items to the receiving server and preserve the original transaction history.
6. Confirm the receiving server can open the transferred check and that the releasing server no longer owns the transferred
balance.
7. Verify totals, payment status, discounts, gratuity settings, and alcohol accountability after the transfer.
8. Communicate the handoff directly to the guest when appropriate and identify who will complete service and payment.
9. Escalate duplicate checks, locked checks, prior payments, tip disputes, or transfer errors to a manager before either employee
checks out.
10. Record the transfer reason and approval when required and include the check in both servers' checkout review.


120. POS Check Transfer Between Servers SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the authorized user, transaction record, approval requirement, custody point, and exception
trigger for POS check transfer between servers.
□ Obtain required approval before transferring ownership, particularly after payment activity, discounts, alcohol service, tip
entry, or shift checkout has begun.
□ Select the approved POS check-transfer or server-transfer function; do not share login credentials or ring future items
under the wrong employee.
□ Confirm the receiving server can open the transferred check and that the releasing server no longer owns the
transferred balance.
□ Communicate the handoff directly to the guest when appropriate and identify who will complete service and payment.
□ Record the transfer reason and approval when required and include the check in both servers' checkout review.
• Transferring a check without direct communication between releasing and receiving servers.
• Sharing employee login credentials or continuing to ring items under the wrong server.
• Moving the wrong seats or losing payment, discount, gratuity, or alcohol accountability.
• Allowing either server to check out before transfer discrepancies are resolved.
• Treating a server transfer as a table move and changing the guest location unnecessarily.

121. POS Split Check SOP

SOP ID V1-121 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To split POS checks accurately while preserving items, taxes, discounts, gratuities, payments, and
guest-approved allocation.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Identify the employee, guest check, drawer, payment, item, amount, and reason connected to POS split check.
2. Use only individual credentials and confirm the correct system, business date, location, and assigned custody.
3. Review the original ticket, receipt, authorization, balance, or supporting record before moving value.
4. Clarify whether the split is by seat, item, amount, percentage, or payment method before changing the check.
5. Assign every item, shared charge, tax, service charge, promotion, and tip line to the correct resulting check.
6. Review subtotals and explain any item that cannot be divided under policy before payment.
7. Process one payment at a time, return receipts correctly, and verify no balance remains.
8. Stop for manager review when identity, amount, authority, payment data, cash count, or audit information does not agree.
9. Verify the final balance, receipt, tender, reason code, custody, and system status.
10. Secure funds and records and document approvals, variances, disputes, and unusual activity.

RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.

123. POS System Down SOP

SOP ID V1-123 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To maintain controlled ordering, payment, kitchen communication, and reconciliation when the POS
system is unavailable.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide

• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Identify the employee, guest check, drawer, payment, item, amount, and reason connected to POS system down.
2. Use only individual credentials and confirm the correct system, business date, location, and assigned custody.
3. Review the original ticket, receipt, authorization, balance, or supporting record before moving value.
4. Confirm the outage scope and stop repeated actions that may duplicate orders or payments.
5. Activate the downtime kit and record table, server, time, items, prices, modifiers, and payment status manually.
6. Route orders through the designated kitchen and bar backup method with numbered accountability.
7. When systems return, enter or reconcile every transaction once and document discrepancies.
8. Stop for manager review when identity, amount, authority, payment data, cash count, or audit information does not agree.
9. Verify the final balance, receipt, tender, reason code, custody, and system status.
10. Secure funds and records and document approvals, variances, disputes, and unusual activity.

124. Cash Drawer Setup SOP

SOP ID V1-124 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To establish each cash drawer with an approved starting bank, verified denomination count, secure
assignment, and documented custody.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Confirm the assigned drawer, employee, business date, location, shift, and approved starting bank.
2. Obtain funds through the approved custody process and move them only in the designated secure area.
3. Count each denomination using the approved count method and verify the total independently.
4. Compare the actual bank with the authorized amount and stop if any variance exists.
5. Record the count, employee, time, drawer identifier, and second verification when required.
6. Load denominations in the approved arrangement without exposing funds to guests or unauthorized employees.
7. Verify receipt paper, terminal assignment, tender keys, cash-drop supplies, and drawer security.
8. Transfer custody directly to the assigned employee and prohibit sharing or unattended access.
9. Report excess, shortage, damaged currency, counterfeit concern, or incorrect access before opening.
10. Secure the drawer and retain the setup record for reconciliation.


124. Cash Drawer Setup SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the authorized user, transaction record, approval requirement, custody point, and exception
trigger for cash drawer setup.
□ Obtain funds through the approved custody process and move them only in the designated secure area.
□ Compare the actual bank with the authorized amount and stop if any variance exists.
□ Load denominations in the approved arrangement without exposing funds to guests or unauthorized employees.
□ Transfer custody directly to the assigned employee and prohibit sharing or unattended access.
□ Secure the drawer and retain the setup record for reconciliation.
• Loading a drawer before the employee, business date, starting bank, and custody are verified.
• Counting in public, sharing the drawer, or leaving it unattended.
• Ignoring an opening variance or forcing the bank to match with unrecorded funds.
• Failing to document denomination count, witness, drawer identifier, and transfer of custody.
• Opening for service before cash, receipt paper, terminal, and drawer security are verified.

125. Cash Drawer Review SOP

SOP ID V1-125 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To review cash drawers for correct balance, transaction support, authorized adjustments, secure
custody, and documented variances.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Identify the drawer, assigned employee, shift, starting bank, sales period, drops, refunds, paid-outs, and authorized
movements.
2. Secure the drawer in the approved count area with required employee or witness presence.
3. Count all cash and noncash items using the approved sequence and recount unusual denominations or bundles.
4. Compare the actual count with the POS expected amount and supporting transaction records.
5. Review drops, refunds, voids, comps, paid-outs, gift cards, and drawer transfers before classifying a variance.
6. Recount and verify the business date, employee, terminal, and tender report when totals do not agree.
7. Document the exact overage, shortage, explanation, employees present, and manager action.
8. Do not replace funds, borrow from another drawer, alter transactions, or ask an employee to repay outside policy.
9. Transfer funds and records into approved custody and restrict access after the count.
10. Sign the review and route repeated or material variances for authorized investigation and follow-up.


125. Cash Drawer Review SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the authorized user, transaction record, approval requirement, custody point, and exception
trigger for cash drawer review.
□ Secure the drawer in the approved count area with required employee or witness presence.
□ Compare the actual count with the POS expected amount and supporting transaction records.
□ Recount and verify the business date, employee, terminal, and tender report when totals do not agree.
□ Do not replace funds, borrow from another drawer, alter transactions, or ask an employee to repay outside policy.
□ Sign the review and route repeated or material variances for authorized investigation and follow-up.
• Counting before all drops, refunds, paid-outs, transfers, and transactions are identified.
• Replacing, borrowing, or moving funds to hide a variance.
• Classifying a shortage or overage without recounting and verifying reports.
• Discussing the count publicly or allowing unauthorized access.
• Failing to document the variance, custody transfer, trend review, and authorized follow-up.

128. Void Approval SOP

SOP ID V1-128 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To approve voids only for verified reasons, within assigned authority, with supporting documentation
and preserved transaction history.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Review the original item, quantity, price, table, employee, time, and stated reason for the requested void.
2. Confirm whether the item was never produced, entered incorrectly, duplicated, cancelled, or otherwise meets the void policy.
3. Compare the request with kitchen/bar production, ticket status, guest communication, and any replacement item.
4. Require the requesting employee to provide factual notes and select the correct approved reason code.
5. Inspect or account for prepared product when policy requires and route waste separately from the financial void.
6. Approve only within assigned authority; escalate high-value, repeated, late, or suspicious requests.
7. Complete the void under the manager's individual credentials without sharing access.
8. Verify the guest check, tax, tip, payment, inventory, and reporting effect after the void.
9. Document coaching, system error, product loss, or investigation follow-up when indicated.
10. Review void trends by employee, item, reason, and shift during reconciliation.

CUSTOMIZATION

policy.
□ Require the requesting employee to provide factual notes and select the correct approved reason code.

□ Verify the guest check, tax, tip, payment, inventory, and reporting effect after the void.
□ Review void trends by employee, item, reason, and shift during reconciliation.
• Treating a produced item, waste, discount, comp, or refund as a void.
• Approving without the original ticket, factual reason, employee explanation, and production status.
• Using shared credentials or the wrong reason code.
• Ignoring high-value, repeated, late, or suspicious void patterns.
• Failing to verify the guest check, inventory, tax, payment, and end-of-day report effect.

129. Comp Approval SOP

SOP ID V1-129 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To approve comps only for authorized business reasons, within established limits, with accurate guest-
check and manager documentation.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Identify the guest, check, item, amount, complaint, and employee requesting the comp.
2. Verify the guest concern and distinguish a service-recovery comp from a void, discount, refund, spill, or employee meal.
3. Confirm the proposed recovery is proportionate and within the manager's authority.
4. Select the correct comp item and reason code and enter factual notes without private or inflammatory language.
5. Obtain higher approval before exceeding limits or compensating restricted items such as alcohol where prohibited.
6. Explain the recovery to the guest without implying additional promises or admitting liability.
7. Apply the comp under individual credentials and verify tax, tip, balance, and payment status.
8. Preserve product, check, witness, or incident information when the comp relates to safety or a disputed event.
9. Record coaching, quality, timing, or process follow-up when the comp reveals a repeated failure.
10. Review comp patterns during end-of-day reconciliation.


129. Comp Approval SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the authorized user, transaction record, approval requirement, custody point, and exception
trigger for comp approval.
□ Verify the guest concern and distinguish a service-recovery comp from a void, discount, refund, spill, or employee meal.
□ Select the correct comp item and reason code and enter factual notes without private or inflammatory language.
□ Explain the recovery to the guest without implying additional promises or admitting liability.
□ Preserve product, check, witness, or incident information when the comp relates to safety or a disputed event.
□ Review comp patterns during end-of-day reconciliation.
• Using a comp when the correct action is a void, discount, refund, spill, or employee meal.
• Comping before verifying the guest concern and manager authority.
• Compensating restricted items or exceeding limits without higher approval.
• Entering vague, private, or inflammatory notes instead of factual reason codes.
• Failing to connect repeated comps with coaching, quality, timing, or system correction.

• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Confirm the promotion, coupon, eligibility, date, location, items, exclusions, and required guest evidence.
2. Inspect the offer for alteration, duplication, expiration, unauthorized screenshots, or terms that do not match the current
program.
3. Verify whether the offer can be combined with other discounts, gift cards, loyalty rewards, alcohol, or employee benefits.
4. Match the discount to the correct guest check and eligible items before applying it.
5. Obtain manager approval for overrides, exceptions, manual codes, or unclear terms.
6. Apply the approved code under individual credentials and retain or mark the coupon as required.
7. Verify the final price, tax, tip basis, tender, and guest receipt.
8. Explain any denial or limitation respectfully using the offer terms rather than personal judgment.
9. Document fraud concerns, repeated misuse, system problems, or guest disputes.
10. Reconcile manual discounts and retained offers at checkout or end of day.

□ Inspect the offer for alteration, duplication, expiration, unauthorized screenshots, or terms that do not match the current

□ Apply the approved code under individual credentials and retain or mark the coupon as required.
□ Explain any denial or limitation respectfully using the offer terms rather than personal judgment.
□ Reconcile manual discounts and retained offers at checkout or end of day.
• Applying an expired, altered, duplicated, ineligible, or noncombinable offer.
• Discounting the wrong items or guest check.
• Overriding unclear terms without manager approval.
• Failing to retain, mark, or document the offer and manual code as required.
• Ending without verifying final price, tax, tip basis, tender, and misuse concerns.

131. Refund Processing SOP

SOP ID V1-131 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To process refunds through the original or approved payment method with verified authority, supporting
records, and guest communication.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Identify the employee, guest check, drawer, payment, item, amount, and reason connected to refund processing.
2. Use only individual credentials and confirm the correct system, business date, location, and assigned custody.
3. Review the original ticket, receipt, authorization, balance, or supporting record before moving value.
4. Verify the original transaction, guest identity as required, issue, payment method, amount, and prior recovery.
5. Obtain required approval and explain expected processing timing without promising bank-controlled results.
6. Process the refund through the approved system and original method unless policy authorizes otherwise.
7. Provide documentation, secure sensitive records, and reconcile the transaction.
8. Stop for manager review when identity, amount, authority, payment data, cash count, or audit information does not agree.
9. Verify the final balance, receipt, tender, reason code, custody, and system status.
10. Secure funds and records and document approvals, variances, disputes, and unusual activity.

132. Gift Card Sale SOP

SOP ID V1-132 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To sell and activate gift cards accurately while protecting payment information, card inventory,
activation records, and guest receipts.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Confirm the requested gift-card type, amount, activation method, tender, fees, and any purchase limits.
2. Use only approved physical or digital cards from secure inventory.
3. Enter the sale under the correct POS function and verify the amount before activation.
4. Accept payment through the approved tender process and never activate a card without completed payment.
5. Confirm the activation result in the system and on the receipt without exposing the full card number or access code.
6. Provide the guest with the card, receipt, terms, and balance-check instructions.
7. Secure unused cards and record inventory movement when required.
8. Stop and notify the manager if activation fails, the card is damaged, inventory does not agree, or the transaction is disputed.
9. Process corrections or refunds only under the approved gift-card policy and authority.
10. Reconcile gift-card sales, activations, failures, and inventory at the required checkpoint.

133. Gift Card Balance and Redemption SOP

SOP ID V1-133 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify gift-card balances and redemptions accurately while protecting card information, remaining
value, transaction records, and guest trust.
APPLIES TO: Authorized POS, cash, payment, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee uses individual credentials and preserves custody. The manager verifies
authority, records, balances, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related POS, cash, payment, approval, refund, or reconciliation action is required.
• POS transaction and audit reports
• Secure cash, payment, or approval records
• Individual POS or cash credentials
• Guest check, drawer, or transaction record
• Authority and reason-code guide
• Employees use only individual credentials and the correct guest check, drawer, or record.
• Cash, payment data, approvals, and audit records remain secure and traceable.
• No employee alters records or uses personal funds to hide an error or variance.
• Differences and disputed activity are preserved and escalated before adjustment.
1. Ask the guest to present the approved physical or digital gift card and confirm the requested use.
2. Access the gift-card function through individual credentials without writing down or exposing the full card number.
3. Verify the available balance through the approved system before promising payment coverage.
4. Apply the correct amount to the correct guest check and retain any remaining balance in the system.
5. Collect another tender for any unpaid balance and follow split-payment rules.
6. Return the physical card when required and provide a receipt showing the transaction and remaining balance when available.
7. Do not manually alter balances, transfer value, or override a failed redemption without authorization.
8. Stop and escalate damaged, unreadable, inactive, disputed, or suspicious cards.
9. Document system failures, guest disputes, duplicate charges, or manager-approved adjustments.
10. Verify gift-card activity during checkout or end-of-day reconciliation.

136. Manager Opening Walkthrough SOP

SOP ID V1-136 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify the restaurant is ready to open through a complete manager walkthrough of people, food,
equipment, cleanliness, safety, cash, and systems.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before the assigned station, department, or building begins service or changes opening
responsibility.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review manager notes, staffing, reservations, events, weather, deliveries, maintenance holds, and expected business.
2. Inspect the exterior, parking, entrance, lobby, dining room, patio, restrooms, pickup area, and guest routes.
3. Verify lighting, climate, music, phones, reservations, POS, payment devices, alarms, locks, and required utilities.
4. Inspect bar, kitchen access, hand sinks, refrigeration, hot equipment, dish, storage, chemicals, and life-safety routes.
5. Confirm food-safety logs, temperatures, cleaning, product readiness, cash custody, keys, and department opening checklists.
6. Verify staffing assignments, call-offs, training needs, breaks, manager zones, and recovery authority.
7. Assign every correction to a named employee with priority and completion time.
8. Recheck hazards, food safety, cash, access, equipment, and guest-facing conditions before opening.
9. Restrict or delay any operation that cannot meet required safety or legal controls.
10. Sign the opening review and communicate remaining limitations and follow-up.


136. Manager Opening Walkthrough SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The manager begins manager opening walkthrough with current data and direct observation and assigns every
unresolved item to an owner and deadline.
□ Inspect the exterior, parking, entrance, lobby, dining room, patio, restrooms, pickup area, and guest routes.
□ Inspect bar, kitchen access, hand sinks, refrigeration, hot equipment, dish, storage, chemicals, and life-safety routes.
□ Verify staffing assignments, call-offs, training needs, breaks, manager zones, and recovery authority.
□ Recheck hazards, food safety, cash, access, equipment, and guest-facing conditions before opening.
□ Sign the opening review and communicate remaining limitations and follow-up.
• Relying on department sign-offs without physically inspecting critical conditions.
• Opening with unresolved food-safety, fire, access, cash, equipment, or guest-route hazards.
• Giving broad instructions without named owners, deadlines, and rechecks.
• Failing to restrict or delay an operation that cannot meet required controls.
• Signing ready without documenting limitations and next follow-up.

137. Manager Closing Walkthrough SOP

SOP ID V1-137 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify the restaurant is safely and completely closed through a manager walkthrough of guests,
cash, food, equipment, utilities, security, and unfinished work.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: After active service ends and before employees are released or the building is secured.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review active guests, checks, employees, deliveries, incidents, maintenance, and unfinished department work.
2. Walk the exterior, parking, patio, entrance, dining room, restrooms, pickup area, and all guest-accessible spaces.
3. Confirm guests and employees have departed or are accounted for and no one remains in restricted areas.
4. Verify bar, kitchen, dish, storage, refrigeration, chemicals, grease, waste, and food disposition are complete.
5. Confirm cash, deposits, payment devices, keys, sensitive records, gift cards, and controlled product are secured.
6. Shut down or leave required equipment operating according to approved instructions and document exceptions.
7. Check doors, windows, exits, fire lanes, utilities, lights, climate, alarms, cameras, and locks.
8. Assign and recheck any incomplete cleaning, safety, maintenance, or security item before releasing employees.
9. Record unresolved conditions, service calls, incidents, and next-shift priorities in the manager log.
10. Complete final lock-up, arm the alarm, and transfer custody according to the keyholder process.

□ Walk the exterior, parking, patio, entrance, dining room, restrooms, pickup area, and all guest-accessible spaces.

• Locking or arming without checking all rooms, exits, contractors, and remaining employees.
• Leaving unresolved cleaning, maintenance, incident, or security items undocumented.
• Failing to transfer final custody and next-shift priorities.

138. Pre-Shift Meeting SOP

SOP ID V1-138 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To align the team before service on business levels, reservations, menu changes, staffing, safety,
priorities, standards, and individual assignments.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled management checkpoint and whenever operating conditions require an additional
review.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review current staffing, guest, sales, ticket, safety, cash, and department information relevant to pre-shift meeting.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Gather staffing, reservations, forecast, menu features, shortages, events, safety concerns, and prior-shift follow-up.
5. Begin on time, confirm attendance, and communicate the most important guest, operational, quality, and sales priorities.
6. Review one relevant SOP or training point and verify understanding through questions or repeat-back.
7. Assign action items and release employees with clear positions, start tasks, and escalation contacts.
8. Assign corrections to named employees with priority and completion times and communicate cross-department effects.
9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
10. Record material decisions, exceptions, recoveries, and follow-up for the next manager or review cycle.

CUSTOMIZATION

□ Gather staffing, reservations, forecast, menu features, shortages, events, safety concerns, and prior-shift follow-up.

□ Assign action items and release employees with clear positions, start tasks, and escalation contacts.
□ Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
• Making a decision about pre-shift meeting from reports alone without observing actual conditions.
• Giving a general instruction without a named owner, priority, completion time, and recheck.
• Allowing guest, safety, staffing, cash, or quality risks to remain unassigned.
• Failing to communicate cross-department effects or changing conditions.
• Ending the review without documenting material decisions, exceptions, and follow-up.

• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review current staffing, guest, sales, ticket, safety, cash, and department information relevant to floor plan and section
assignment.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Review open tables, reservations, party sizes, accessibility needs, server skill, arrival times, and support staffing.
5. Create balanced sections using seat count, distance, workload, and expected turns rather than preference alone.
6. Assign host, server, assistant, runner, bartender, and manager coverage and mark closed or reserved areas.
7. Communicate the map, update systems, obtain acknowledgment, and document later changes.
8. Assign corrections to named employees with priority and completion times and communicate cross-department effects.
9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
10. Record material decisions, exceptions, recoveries, and follow-up for the next manager or review cycle.

140. Break Coverage SOP

SOP ID V1-140 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To schedule and execute employee breaks without leaving guests, stations, cash, safety duties, or
required positions uncovered.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled management checkpoint and whenever operating conditions require an additional
review.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review current staffing, guest, sales, ticket, safety, cash, and department information relevant to break coverage.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Review business volume, required timing, position criticality, and available trained coverage.
5. Assign a named qualified employee to every table, station, cash, phone, safety, or production responsibility.
6. Conduct a direct handoff of active guests, tickets, payments, allergies, shortages, and unresolved issues.
7. Record the break, monitor return, restore the assignment, and address missed or interrupted breaks through policy.
8. Assign corrections to named employees with priority and completion times and communicate cross-department effects.
9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
10. Record material decisions, exceptions, recoveries, and follow-up for the next manager or review cycle.

APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.

143. Rush Readiness Check SOP

SOP ID V1-143 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To confirm the restaurant, kitchen, bar, host stand, staffing, supplies, and communication plan are
ready before a predicted rush.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled management checkpoint and whenever operating conditions require an additional
review.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.

• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review current staffing, guest, sales, ticket, safety, cash, and department information relevant to rush readiness check.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Review reservations, weather, events, sales pace, staffing, call-offs, ticket load, and known constraints.

6. Confirm employee positions, break plan, manager zones, recovery authority, and kitchen-floor communication.

9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.

144. Service Recovery Leadership SOP

SOP ID V1-144 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To lead service recovery consistently by confirming facts, protecting safety, selecting authorized action,
supporting employees, and verifying guest follow-up.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled management checkpoint and whenever operating conditions require an additional
review.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review current staffing, guest, sales, ticket, safety, cash, and department information relevant to service recovery leadership.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Take ownership of communication, gather facts quickly, and assign one leader as the guest contact.
5. Stop ongoing errors and coordinate kitchen, bar, server, host, and payment corrections with realistic timing.
6. Authorize proportionate recovery within policy and ensure all employees communicate the same plan.
7. Follow up with the guest, document cause and outcome, coach privately, and assign prevention action.
8. Assign corrections to named employees with priority and completion times and communicate cross-department effects.
9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
10. Record material decisions, exceptions, recoveries, and follow-up for the next manager or review cycle.

walkthrough.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Walk exterior, entrance, host, dining, bar, restrooms, service stations, visible kitchen, and pickup zones.
5. Check floors, tables, touchpoints, trash, odors, lighting, temperatures, music, uniforms, food presentation, and clutter.
6. Correct immediate hazards personally or assign a named employee while protecting guests from the area.
7. Recheck completion and document maintenance or repeated failures.
8. Assign corrections to named employees with priority and completion times and communicate cross-department effects.
9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
10. Record material decisions, exceptions, recoveries, and follow-up for the next manager or review cycle.

□ Correct immediate hazards personally or assign a named employee while protecting guests from the area.
□ Recheck completion and document maintenance or repeated failures.
□ Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
• Making a decision about quality and cleanliness walkthrough from reports alone without observing actual conditions.
• Giving a general instruction without a named owner, priority, completion time, and recheck.
• Allowing guest, safety, staffing, cash, or quality risks to remain unassigned.
• Failing to communicate cross-department effects or changing conditions.
• Ending the review without documenting material decisions, exceptions, and follow-up.

149. Manager Table Visit SOP

SOP ID V1-149 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To conduct purposeful manager table visits that confirm guest satisfaction, identify concerns early, and
support the service team without interrupting the meal.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled management checkpoint and whenever operating conditions require an additional
review.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review current staffing, guest, sales, ticket, safety, cash, and department information relevant to manager table visit.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Review table, server, order status, special occasion, allergy, delay, or recovery reason before approaching.
5. Introduce yourself, ask a focused open question, and observe food, beverages, table condition, and guest response.
6. Address simple needs immediately and take ownership of concerns.
7. Inform the server, complete promised action, and document serious or recurring feedback.
8. Assign corrections to named employees with priority and completion times and communicate cross-department effects.
9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
10. Record material decisions, exceptions, recoveries, and follow-up for the next manager or review cycle.

150. Manager Cash Drawer Review SOP

SOP ID V1-150 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To review manager-controlled cash drawers for correct balance, authorization, supporting records,
custody, and timely investigation of variances.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled management checkpoint and whenever operating conditions require an additional
review.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review current staffing, guest, sales, ticket, safety, cash, and department information relevant to manager cash drawer review.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Confirm the assigned employee, drawer, starting bank, sales period, drops, paid-outs, refunds, and authorized movements.
5. Count cash in the approved secure area with required employee or second-manager presence.
6. Compare actual funds to expected cash and review transaction records before concluding a variance exists.
7. Recount, document explanations, secure funds and evidence, and follow the approved variance process.
8. Assign corrections to named employees with priority and completion times and communicate cross-department effects.
9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
10. Record material decisions, exceptions, recoveries, and follow-up for the next manager or review cycle.

151. Manager Incident Documentation SOP

SOP ID V1-151 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To document incidents with objective facts, protected information, required evidence, notifications,
corrective action, and assigned follow-up.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review current staffing, guest, sales, ticket, safety, cash, and department information relevant to manager incident
documentation.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Secure immediate safety, preserve the scene or records, and identify the reporting manager.
5. Collect separate statements, witness contacts, times, locations, checks, photographs, and system information as permitted.
6. Write a chronological account distinguishing direct observation from reported information.
7. Record actions and notifications, sign and secure the report, and assign follow-up.
8. Assign corrections to named employees with priority and completion times and communicate cross-department effects.
9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
10. Record material decisions, exceptions, recoveries, and follow-up for the next manager or review cycle.
