152. Manager Shift Handoff SOP

SOP ID V1-152 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To transfer complete operational status, guest issues, cash and key custody, staffing, equipment, safety
concerns, and unfinished priorities between managers.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever responsibility, custody, information, or an active order transfers between employees.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review current staffing, guest, sales, ticket, safety, cash, and department information relevant to manager shift handoff.
2. Walk or observe the actual operation and compare conditions to the current approved standard.
3. Identify hazards, guest-impacting delays, capacity limits, and work that lacks a clear owner.
4. Review staffing, call-offs, breaks, labor, reservations, sales pace, ticket times, shortages, and department status.
5. Identify active complaints, allergy or safety concerns, incidents, employee issues, vendors, maintenance, and pending
decisions.
6. Transfer keys, safe status, deposits, drawers, devices, documents, and system access according to policy.
7. Both managers confirm priorities, owners, due times, risks, and the written handoff.
8. Assign corrections to named employees with priority and completion times and communicate cross-department effects.
9. Recheck high-risk items and adjust the plan when conditions change or results remain below standard.
10. Record material decisions, exceptions, recoveries, and follow-up for the next manager or review cycle.

APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.

154. Manager Daily SOP Compliance Review SOP

SOP ID V1-154 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify daily compliance with selected high-risk and high-frequency SOPs, document exceptions,
assign correction, and confirm retraining when needed.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled management checkpoint and whenever operating conditions require an additional
review.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Review the shift's assigned SOP compliance priorities, high-risk tasks, prior exceptions, and outstanding corrective actions
before the first walkthrough.
2. Observe employees performing selected procedures in real time rather than relying only on completed checklists or verbal
assurances.
3. Compare observed actions with the current approved SOP, including sequence, safety controls, required documentation, and
escalation points.
4. Verify critical records such as temperatures, cash approvals, cleaning checks, training sign-offs, or opening/closing tasks
against observable conditions.
5. Correct immediate safety, food-safety, security, or financial-control failures at once and stop the task when continuation would
create risk.
6. Coach minor execution gaps using the exact standard, then require the employee to repeat or demonstrate the corrected step.
7. Record the SOP reviewed, employees or area observed, result, exception, correction, and responsible follow-up owner.
8. Look for patterns across employees, shifts, equipment, workload, or unclear instructions instead of treating every miss as
individual carelessness.
9. Escalate repeated, serious, or system-wide noncompliance according to the restaurant's accountability and retraining process.
10. Close the daily review by confirming urgent corrections are complete and carrying unresolved items into the manager log and
next-shift handoff.


154. Manager Daily SOP Compliance Review SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The manager begins manager daily SOP compliance review with current data and direct observation and assigns every
unresolved item to an owner and deadline.
□ Observe employees performing selected procedures in real time rather than relying only on completed checklists or
verbal assurances.
□ Verify critical records such as temperatures, cash approvals, cleaning checks, training sign-offs, or opening/closing
tasks against observable conditions.
□ Coach minor execution gaps using the exact standard, then require the employee to repeat or demonstrate the
corrected step.
□ Look for patterns across employees, shifts, equipment, workload, or unclear instructions instead of treating every miss
as individual carelessness.
□ Close the daily review by confirming urgent corrections are complete and carrying unresolved items into the manager
log and next-shift handoff.
• Reviewing only paperwork and never observing the procedure being performed.
• Treating every missed step as an employee problem without checking training, tools, workload, or clarity.
• Delaying correction of food-safety, safety, cash-control, or security failures.
• Recording vague findings that do not identify the SOP, exception, owner, and follow-up.
• Ending the shift without carrying unresolved compliance risks into the manager handoff.

155. Manager Weekly SOP Review SOP

SOP ID V1-155 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To review SOP performance weekly, identify recurring failures or outdated procedures, assign
revisions, and verify training and document control.
APPLIES TO: Managers, shift leads, department leads, and employees receiving assignments.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager reviews conditions, assigns owners and deadlines, verifies completion, and
documents material exceptions.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled management checkpoint and whenever operating conditions require an additional
review.
• Manager checklist or log
• Staffing, sales, reservation, and ticket information
• Department status and performance records
• Communication and assignment tools
• Incident and follow-up records
• The manager uses current data and direct observation rather than assumptions.
• Every correction or priority has a named owner, deadline, and verification point.
• Guest, employee, food-safety, cash, alcohol, and building risks are escalated promptly.
• Material decisions and unresolved issues are recorded for the next leader.
1. Gather the week's daily compliance reviews, incident reports, retraining records, audit results, guest complaints, safety events,
and manager logs.
2. Group findings by SOP, department, shift, employee role, risk level, and recurrence so isolated errors are separated from
operating patterns.
3. Verify that every high-risk exception received immediate containment and that promised corrective actions were completed.
4. Identify procedures that are unclear, outdated, impractical, poorly trained, inconsistently supervised, or affected by changed
equipment or policy.
5. Review completion and quality trends for required logs, checklists, approvals, and sign-offs rather than counting paperwork
alone.
6. Select the highest-priority SOPs for observation, coaching, retraining, revision, or leadership follow-up during the coming
week.
7. Assign a named owner, due date, verification method, and escalation threshold for each corrective action.
8. Communicate relevant findings to department leaders without exposing confidential employee information or using the review
to shame individuals.
9. Update the master SOP review or revision record when a procedure requires formal change, approval, version control, and
retraining.
10. Document the weekly conclusion, unresolved risks, next review date, and items that must be reported to ownership or senior
leadership.

□ The manager begins manager weekly SOP review with current data and direct observation and assigns every
unresolved item to an owner and deadline.
□ Group findings by SOP, department, shift, employee role, risk level, and recurrence so isolated errors are separated

□ Identify procedures that are unclear, outdated, impractical, poorly trained, inconsistently supervised, or affected by
changed equipment or policy.
□ Select the highest-priority SOPs for observation, coaching, retraining, revision, or leadership follow-up during the
coming week.
□ Communicate relevant findings to department leaders without exposing confidential employee information or using the
review to shame individuals.
□ Document the weekly conclusion, unresolved risks, next review date, and items that must be reported to ownership or
senior leadership.
• Counting completed forms without evaluating whether the standards were actually met.
• Focusing on isolated mistakes while missing repeated patterns across shifts or departments.
• Assigning corrective actions without owners, deadlines, or verification methods.
• Revising an SOP informally without approval, version control, and employee retraining.
• Sharing confidential employee details unnecessarily during operational review.

PART 8

EMPLOYEE COMMUNICATION, CONDUCT
& DOCUMENTATION

15 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs create consistent attendance, coaching, conduct, complaint, accommodation, training, and
recognition practices.


156. Employee Call-Off SOP

SOP ID V1-156 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To receive employee call-offs consistently, document required facts, protect privacy, assess coverage,
and apply attendance policy through authorized management.
APPLIES TO: Employees, trainers, managers, and authorized human-resources or ownership contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager responds privately and consistently, records objective facts, and routes protected
or formal matters to authorized leadership.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related employee communication, conduct, scheduling, training, or documentation
need occurs.
• Current schedule and call-off policy
• Approved employee contact method
• Employee, shift, and position information
• Qualified coverage roster
• Call-off documentation log
• Managers address specific facts and the applicable standard, not personality, rumor, or diagnosis.
• Conversations are private, respectful, consistent, and documented at the proper level.
• Good-faith complaints, protected requests, and review participation are not retaliated against.
• Safety, harassment, accommodation, and job-status decisions are routed to authorized leadership.
1. Review the current schedule, policy, SOP, training record, and facts relevant to employee call-off.
2. Meet or communicate through the approved channel and protect privacy from guests and uninvolved employees.
3. State or request specific facts and listen without labels, rumors, ridicule, diagnosis, or premature conclusions.
4. Record employee, shift, position, call time, approved contact method, and stated availability information.
5. Avoid unnecessary medical details and explain required documentation or next steps under policy.
6. Notify management, update the schedule, and begin qualified coverage without making premature promises.
7. Confirm receipt, record attempts and decisions, and route protected-leave questions appropriately.
8. Explain the next operational step, expected conduct, coverage, training, or authorized review process.
9. Document necessary facts and route protected, formal, safety, or job-status matters to authorized leadership.
10. Set a follow-up owner and time and verify coverage, nonretaliation, training, or corrective commitments.


156. Employee Call-Off SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The manager can state the applicable policy, privacy rule, documentation level, routing authority, and nonretaliation
requirement for employee call-off.
□ Record employee, shift, position, call time, approved contact method, and stated availability information.
□ Avoid unnecessary medical details and explain required documentation or next steps under policy.
□ Notify management, update the schedule, and begin qualified coverage without making premature promises.
□ Confirm receipt, record attempts and decisions, and route protected-leave questions appropriately.
□ Document necessary facts and route protected, formal, safety, or job-status matters to authorized leadership.
• Addressing employee call-off publicly or relying on rumor, labels, personality judgments, or unnecessary medical details.
• Applying a standard inconsistently or making a job-status promise outside assigned authority.
• Failing to protect good-faith reporting, accommodation routing, privacy, or nonretaliation requirements.
• Documenting conclusions instead of specific observed or reported facts and employee response.
• Ending the conversation without a clear operational next step, owner, timing, and follow-up.
• Employee policy, handbook section, training standard, or reporting channel: ______________________
• Schedule, coaching, incident, accommodation, or corrective-action form: _________________________
• Manager, HR, ownership, or protected-reporting escalation role: _________________________________
• Notice, response, documentation, review, or follow-up target: ___________________________________
• Employment, leave, accommodation, harassment, privacy, or nonretaliation requirement reviewed: __
• Restaurant-specific conduct, coverage, training, or recognition steps: _________________________

157. Late Arrival Response SOP

SOP ID V1-157 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To respond to late arrivals consistently by protecting immediate coverage, documenting objective facts,
confirming expectations, and applying policy fairly.
APPLIES TO: Employees, trainers, managers, and authorized human-resources or ownership contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager responds privately and consistently, records objective facts, and routes protected
or formal matters to authorized leadership.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Current schedule and attendance policy
• Approved employee contact methods
• Timekeeping record
• Temporary coverage plan
• Attendance coaching form
• Managers address specific facts and the applicable standard, not personality, rumor, or diagnosis.
• Conversations are private, respectful, consistent, and documented at the proper level.
• Good-faith complaints, protected requests, and review participation are not retaliated against.
• Safety, harassment, accommodation, and job-status decisions are routed to authorized leadership.
1. Review the current schedule, policy, SOP, training record, and facts relevant to late arrival response.
2. Meet or communicate through the approved channel and protect privacy from guests and uninvolved employees.
3. State or request specific facts and listen without labels, rumors, ridicule, diagnosis, or premature conclusions.
4. Attempt contact through the approved method and record scheduled time, actual contact, reason, and expected arrival.
5. Arrange temporary coverage for tables, station, cash, opening, or safety duties.
6. Meet privately on arrival, confirm readiness, explain impact and the attendance standard, and assign the position.
7. Correct time records and document coaching or escalation consistently.
8. Explain the next operational step, expected conduct, coverage, training, or authorized review process.
9. Document necessary facts and route protected, formal, safety, or job-status matters to authorized leadership.
10. Set a follow-up owner and time and verify coverage, nonretaliation, training, or corrective commitments.

or formal matters to authorized leadership.

PURPOSE: To approve or deny shift swaps only after verifying employee qualifications, overtime impact, coverage,

APPLIES TO: Employees, trainers, managers, and authorized human-resources or ownership contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager responds privately and consistently, records objective facts, and routes protected
or formal matters to authorized leadership.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related employee communication, conduct, scheduling, training, or documentation
need occurs.

• Shift-swap request form
• Position-training and readiness-verification record
• Availability and hour-limit information
• Manager approval record
• Managers address specific facts and the applicable standard, not personality, rumor, or diagnosis.
• Conversations are private, respectful, consistent, and documented at the proper level.
• Good-faith complaints, protected requests, and review participation are not retaliated against.

4. Require the request through the approved method with employees, shifts, positions, and dates clearly identified.
5. Verify agreement, training, availability, hour limits, and absence of unapproved conflicts.
6. Obtain manager approval before the change is treated as final and update the official schedule.

162. Employee Coaching SOP

SOP ID V1-162 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To coach employees privately with clear facts, expected standards, practical instruction, documented
commitments, and scheduled follow-up.
APPLIES TO: Employees, trainers, managers, and authorized human-resources or ownership contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager responds privately and consistently, records objective facts, and routes protected
or formal matters to authorized leadership.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related employee communication, conduct, scheduling, training, or documentation
need occurs.
• Relevant SOP or performance standard
• Specific observed facts
• Private coaching area
• Demonstration or practice tools
• Coaching follow-up form
• Managers address specific facts and the applicable standard, not personality, rumor, or diagnosis.
• Conversations are private, respectful, consistent, and documented at the proper level.
• Good-faith complaints, protected requests, and review participation are not retaliated against.
• Safety, harassment, accommodation, and job-status decisions are routed to authorized leadership.
1. Review the current schedule, policy, SOP, training record, and facts relevant to employee coaching.
2. Meet or communicate through the approved channel and protect privacy from guests and uninvolved employees.
3. State or request specific facts and listen without labels, rumors, ridicule, diagnosis, or premature conclusions.
4. Prepare specific observed facts, the relevant SOP or expectation, and the desired outcome.
5. Ask for the employee perspective and clarify misunderstanding without labels, rumor, or public criticism.
6. Explain the gap and impact, demonstrate or practice the correct behavior, and agree on measurable next steps.
7. Document at the required level, set follow-up, and recognize improvement when verified.
8. Explain the next operational step, expected conduct, coverage, training, or authorized review process.
9. Document necessary facts and route protected, formal, safety, or job-status matters to authorized leadership.
10. Set a follow-up owner and time and verify coverage, nonretaliation, training, or corrective commitments.

RESPONSIBILITY: The manager responds privately and consistently, records objective facts, and routes protected

• Prior coaching and performance records

164. Employee Conflict Response SOP

SOP ID V1-164 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To respond to employee conflict by stabilizing operations, hearing each person fairly, protecting safety
and privacy, and assigning appropriate follow-up.
APPLIES TO: Employees, trainers, managers, and authorized human-resources or ownership contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager responds privately and consistently, records objective facts, and routes protected
or formal matters to authorized leadership.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Workplace conduct standard
• Immediate coverage plan
• Private meeting area
• Separate fact-gathering notes
• Security, HR, or ownership contacts
• Managers address specific facts and the applicable standard, not personality, rumor, or diagnosis.
• Conversations are private, respectful, consistent, and documented at the proper level.
• Good-faith complaints, protected requests, and review participation are not retaliated against.
• Safety, harassment, accommodation, and job-status decisions are routed to authorized leadership.
1. Review the current schedule, policy, SOP, training record, and facts relevant to employee conflict response.
2. Meet or communicate through the approved channel and protect privacy from guests and uninvolved employees.
3. State or request specific facts and listen without labels, rumors, ridicule, diagnosis, or premature conclusions.
4. Separate immediate arguments or unsafe conduct and restore operational coverage.
5. Meet privately, usually separately first, and gather specific facts and desired work-related outcomes.
6. Restate conduct and communication expectations without forcing personal agreement.
7. Create a follow-up plan and escalate threats, harassment, retaliation, or repeated disruption.
8. Explain the next operational step, expected conduct, coverage, training, or authorized review process.
9. Document necessary facts and route protected, formal, safety, or job-status matters to authorized leadership.
10. Set a follow-up owner and time and verify coverage, nonretaliation, training, or corrective commitments.

166. Harassment Complaint Intake SOP

SOP ID V1-166 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To receive harassment complaints seriously and privately, protect against retaliation, preserve facts,
route the report correctly, and avoid unauthorized investigation.
APPLIES TO: All guest-facing employees, shift leads, managers, and designated emergency or follow-up contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect immediate safety and notify management. The manager controls response,
recovery, records, and authorized follow-up.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the concern, report, unsafe condition, or incident is observed or received.
• Harassment complaint intake form
• Emergency and security contacts
• Private communication area
• Nonretaliation and reporting notice
• Authorized HR or ownership routing
• Immediate safety and operational control come before recovery or paperwork.
• Employees do not diagnose, admit liability, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
• The scene, product, transaction, and witness information are preserved when required.
• Objective documentation and authorized follow-up are completed promptly.
1. Protect immediate safety and notify the manager before beginning the formal harassment complaint intake response.
2. Contact emergency, security, law-enforcement, HR, ownership, or other approved resources when required.
3. Preserve the scene, item, check, records, and other evidence without creating additional risk.
4. Listen without blame or promises and determine whether immediate safety action is needed.
5. Record the employee words, people involved, dates, locations, witnesses, evidence, and preferred contact.
6. Explain limited need-to-know privacy and the nonretaliation expectation.
7. Notify the designated manager, owner, or HR contact promptly and preserve records without unauthorized investigation.
8. Collect separate witness names and distinguish direct observations from information reported by others.
9. Do not diagnose, admit liability, assign unsupported blame, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
10. Complete, sign, secure, and route the report promptly and assign authorized follow-up.


166. Harassment Complaint Intake SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The manager can state the applicable policy, privacy rule, documentation level, routing authority, and nonretaliation
requirement for harassment complaint intake.
□ Listen without blame or promises and determine whether immediate safety action is needed.
□ Record the employee words, people involved, dates, locations, witnesses, evidence, and preferred contact.
□ Explain limited need-to-know privacy and the nonretaliation expectation.
□ Notify the designated manager, owner, or HR contact promptly and preserve records without unauthorized
investigation.
□ Do not diagnose, admit liability, assign unsupported blame, threaten, or promise outcomes outside authority.
• Delaying immediate safety action while gathering details or discussing recovery for harassment complaint intake.
• Diagnosing, admitting liability, assigning blame, or promising an outcome outside authority.
• Combining witness accounts or failing to preserve the item, scene, check, or records.
• Discussing private guest or employee information with uninvolved people.
• Failing to complete, secure, route, and assign follow-up for the incident record.

167. Workplace Accommodation Request Routing
SOP

SOP ID V1-167 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To route workplace accommodation requests respectfully and confidentially to the authorized decision-
maker without promising, denying, or diagnosing.
APPLIES TO: Employees, trainers, managers, and authorized human-resources or ownership contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: The manager responds privately and consistently, records objective facts, and routes protected
or formal matters to authorized leadership.
WHEN USED: Whenever the related employee communication, conduct, scheduling, training, or documentation
need occurs.
• Accommodation request routing form
• Current role and essential-duty information
• Private communication method
• Authorized HR or ownership contact
• Temporary safety or scheduling plan
• Managers address specific facts and the applicable standard, not personality, rumor, or diagnosis.
• Conversations are private, respectful, consistent, and documented at the proper level.
• Good-faith complaints, protected requests, and review participation are not retaliated against.
• Safety, harassment, accommodation, and job-status decisions are routed to authorized leadership.
1. Review the current schedule, policy, SOP, training record, and facts relevant to workplace accommodation request routing.
2. Meet or communicate through the approved channel and protect privacy from guests and uninvolved employees.
3. State or request specific facts and listen without labels, rumors, ridicule, diagnosis, or premature conclusions.
4. Listen and document the requested work change or limitation without demanding a diagnosis or debating eligibility.
5. Identify the authorized manager, owner, or HR contact responsible for review.
6. Protect privacy and share only information needed for temporary safety or scheduling.
7. Forward promptly, record the handoff, avoid retaliation, and follow the approved review process.
8. Explain the next operational step, expected conduct, coverage, training, or authorized review process.
9. Document necessary facts and route protected, formal, safety, or job-status matters to authorized leadership.
10. Set a follow-up owner and time and verify coverage, nonretaliation, training, or corrective commitments.


167. Workplace Accommodation Request Routing
SOP - VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Protect privacy and share only information needed for temporary safety or scheduling.
□ Forward promptly, record the handoff, avoid retaliation, and follow the approved review process.
□ Document necessary facts and route protected, formal, safety, or job-status matters to authorized leadership.
• Addressing workplace accommodation request routing publicly or relying on rumor, labels, personality judgments, or
unnecessary medical details.
• Applying a standard inconsistently or making a job-status promise outside assigned authority.
• Failing to protect good-faith reporting, accommodation routing, privacy, or nonretaliation requirements.
• Documenting conclusions instead of specific observed or reported facts and employee response.
• Ending the conversation without a clear operational next step, owner, timing, and follow-up.

169. Trainer Assignment and Handoff SOP

SOP ID V1-169 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To assign the correct trainer and transfer the training plan, completed work, employee needs,
standards, and next verification responsibilities.
APPLIES TO: Employees releasing or receiving the order, station, transaction, or responsibility, plus managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The releasing employee gives complete information. The receiving employee confirms
acceptance; management resolves exceptions.
WHEN USED: Whenever responsibility, custody, information, or an active order transfers between employees.
• Trainer schedule and qualification record
• Employee training record
• Completed skill checklist
• Next-shift training objectives
• Manager handoff record
• The handoff is direct, complete, and acknowledged.
• Allergy, cash, guest, safety, identity, and timing information is repeated back when relevant.
• Responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.
• Every unresolved item has a named owner, deadline, and manager awareness.
1. Identify the exact order, guest, station, transaction, custody, and time period covered by trainer assignment and handoff.
2. Conduct the transfer directly whenever possible and use the approved written or electronic record.
3. Review active work, deadlines, high-risk conditions, and items awaiting manager action.
4. Confirm the trainer is qualified, scheduled, and informed of the employee role and planned tasks.
5. Review completed modules, strengths, safety limits, unfinished practice, questions, and verification results.
6. Set next-shift objectives, demonstration tasks, practice limits, and manager checkpoints.
7. Both trainers and management acknowledge the handoff and update the official training record.
8. Require repeat-back of critical allergy, cash, identity, safety, timing, and guest information.
9. Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
10. Both employees acknowledge the transfer; responsibility remains with the releasing employee until acceptance is clear.


169. Trainer Assignment and Handoff SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The manager can state the applicable policy, privacy rule, documentation level, routing authority, and nonretaliation
requirement for trainer assignment and handoff.
□ Confirm the trainer is qualified, scheduled, and informed of the employee role and planned tasks.
□ Review completed modules, strengths, safety limits, unfinished practice, questions, and verification results.
□ Set next-shift objectives, demonstration tasks, practice limits, and manager checkpoints.
□ Both trainers and management acknowledge the handoff and update the official training record.
□ Assign a named owner and due time for every unresolved item and inform management of exceptions.
• Giving a general verbal update instead of exact identifiers and current status for trainer assignment and handoff.
• Failing to repeat back allergy, identity, cash, timing, or safety information.
• Assuming the receiver accepted responsibility without clear acknowledgment.
• Omitting an unresolved item, named owner, deadline, or manager notification.
• Discarding the source ticket, log, or record before the transfer is verified.

PART 9

OPENING, CLOSING & BUILDING
SECURITY

5 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs coordinate front-of-house opening and closing, final building checks, alarm control, locks, keys,
and access accountability.


171. FOH Opening SOP

SOP ID V1-171 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To open front-of-house areas with verified cleanliness, supplies, equipment, cash controls, staffing,
safety, and manager release.
APPLIES TO: Assigned opening employees, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Assigned employees complete the opening work. The manager or shift lead verifies readiness
before service begins.
WHEN USED: Before the assigned station, department, or building begins service or changes opening
responsibility.
• Current opening checklist
• Approved cleaning and sanitizing supplies
• Required stock and operating tools
• System, equipment, cash, or access information
• Manager communication
• The area is clean, stocked, safe, and operational before service begins.
• Cash, product, keys, systems, and equipment are verified before use.
• Shortages, damage, hazards, and incomplete work are reported before guests are affected.
• Opening is not signed off until management verifies readiness.
1. Review the current FOH opening checklist, expected business, assignments, and manager notes.
2. Gather approved tools and obtain only authorized cash, keys, product, or system access.
3. Inspect the assigned area for damage, contamination, shortages, unsafe conditions, and incomplete prior work.
4. Inspect entrances, exterior approach, lobby, dining, restrooms, bar, patio, pickup, and service stations.
5. Activate or secure lighting, climate, music, POS, phones, reservations, payment, cash, menus, alcohol access, and devices.
6. Verify or complete tables, supplies, cleaning, accessibility routes, staffing, active checks, and department assignments.
7. Correct hazards and shortages, complete sign-offs, and obtain manager authorization to open or proceed to lock-up.
8. Correct and report shortages, equipment problems, unsafe conditions, or incomplete work before service.
9. Complete a final readiness check and demonstrate critical controls requested by management.
10. Record completion and obtain manager or shift-lead release before the area begins service.


171. FOH Opening SOP - VERIFICATION &
CUSTOMIZATION

□ The assigned keyholder confirms authority, people, access devices, security contacts, and unresolved conditions before
FOH opening.
□ Inspect entrances, exterior approach, lobby, dining, restrooms, bar, patio, pickup, and service stations.
□ Activate or secure lighting, climate, music, POS, phones, reservations, payment, cash, menus, alcohol access, and
devices.
□ Verify or complete tables, supplies, cleaning, accessibility routes, staffing, active checks, and department assignments.
□ Correct hazards and shortages, complete sign-offs, and obtain manager authorization to open or proceed to lock-up.
□ Complete a final readiness check and demonstrate critical controls requested by management.
• Starting FOH opening from memory instead of the current checklist and manager notes.
• Opening with a shortage, hazard, unverified system, or incomplete prior-shift work.
• Using unassigned cash, keys, product, credentials, or equipment access.
• Correcting a problem without recording it or informing the responsible manager.
• Signing the area ready before the final manager verification.
• Opening, closing, key, alarm, access, or building-security checklist: ____________________________
• Lock, key, code, alarm, camera, utility, or communication system: _______________________________
• Keyholder, owner, alarm company, security, or emergency contact: ________________________________
• Opening, closing, transfer, inspection, or response target: ____________________________________
• Fire, occupancy, life-safety, accessibility, insurance, or local security requirement reviewed: __
• Restaurant-specific route, access level, custody, or exception steps: ___________________________

172. FOH Closing SOP

SOP ID V1-172 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To close front-of-house areas by completing guest service, cash and key custody, cleaning, restocking,
security, and next-shift preparation.
APPLIES TO: Assigned closing employees, trainers, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Assigned employees complete closing work. The manager verifies sanitation, cash, security,
and unresolved conditions before release.
WHEN USED: After active service ends and before employees are released or the building is secured.
• Current closing checklist
• Approved cleaning and sanitizing supplies
• Storage, waste, and security controls
• Active guest, order, or transaction status
• Manager sign-off
• All guests, transactions, product, cash, equipment, and records are resolved or transferred.
• Cleaning, storage, waste, and security follow approved standards.
• Damage, shortages, incidents, and unfinished work are documented.
• The employee leaves only after management verifies the closing assignment.
1. Review the current FOH closing checklist and confirm management has approved the start of closing work.
2. Identify active guests, orders, checks, cash, controlled product, and duties that must remain available.
3. Gather approved cleaning, storage, waste, and security supplies without obstructing operating areas.
4. Inspect entrances, exterior approach, lobby, dining, restrooms, bar, patio, pickup, and service stations.
5. Activate or secure lighting, climate, music, POS, phones, reservations, payment, cash, menus, alcohol access, and devices.
6. Verify or complete tables, supplies, cleaning, accessibility routes, staffing, active checks, and department assignments.
7. Correct hazards and shortages, complete sign-offs, and obtain manager authorization to open or proceed to lock-up.
8. Report breakage, shortage, maintenance, safety, cash, or guest issues and assign unresolved follow-up.
9. Complete the final checklist and walk the area with the manager or designated verifier.
10. Sign off only after work is accepted and remaining responsibility is formally transferred.

CUSTOMIZATION

□ The assigned keyholder confirms authority, people, access devices, security contacts, and unresolved conditions before
FOH closing.
□ Inspect entrances, exterior approach, lobby, dining, restrooms, bar, patio, pickup, and service stations.
□ Activate or secure lighting, climate, music, POS, phones, reservations, payment, cash, menus, alcohol access, and
devices.
□ Verify or complete tables, supplies, cleaning, accessibility routes, staffing, active checks, and department assignments.
□ Correct hazards and shortages, complete sign-offs, and obtain manager authorization to open or proceed to lock-up.
□ Complete the final checklist and walk the area with the manager or designated verifier.
• Starting FOH closing while guests, checks, orders, or required service functions are still active.
• Mixing cleaning, product storage, cash, waste, and security work without the approved sequence.
• Leaving shortages, damage, maintenance, or safety concerns undocumented.
• Releasing keys, cash, product, or records without confirmed custody.
• Leaving before the manager accepts the closing assignment.

173. End-of-Night Building Check SOP

SOP ID V1-173 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify at the end of night that all people have exited, hazards are controlled, utilities and equipment
are secure, and required records are complete.
APPLIES TO: Authorized managers, keyholders, security employees, and designated leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The authorized manager completes the security sequence, controls credentials, and transfers
unresolved risk to the proper contact.
WHEN USED: After active service ends and before employees are released or the building is secured.
• Building security checklist
• Authorized keys, cards, codes, or alarm access
• Employee and visitor accountability
• Emergency and alarm contacts
• Security or access log
• Only authorized employees use keys, codes, locks, and alarm systems.
• People are accounted for before final locking, arming, or access transfer.
• Unresolved doors, alarms, utilities, hazards, or access losses are not ignored.
• Final checks, custody changes, and exceptions are documented.
1. Confirm authorization and obtain the checklist, keys, access devices, contacts, and accountability information for end-of-night
building check.
2. Account for employees, guests, contractors, active transactions, and conditions that prevent secure closure or access control.
3. Use the approved route and sequence; do not enter or remain in an area that appears unsafe or unlawfully accessed.
4. Inspect dining, bar, kitchen, restrooms, office, storage, coolers, exterior, and hidden areas by the approved route.
5. Confirm no guest, employee, contractor, open flame, active cooking, leak, unsecured chemical, or immediate hazard remains.
6. Check refrigeration, ventilation, doors, windows, gates, cash storage, keys, and systems that must remain on or off.
7. Document unresolved conditions and do not leave until responsibility is transferred.
8. Stop and contact management, alarm company, security, or emergency services when a condition cannot be resolved.
9. Verify locks, alarms, keys, utilities, access, and final custody through physical or system confirmation.
10. Record time, employee, credential, result, and exception before departure or transfer.


173. End-of-Night Building Check SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The assigned keyholder confirms authority, people, access devices, security contacts, and unresolved conditions before
end-of-night building check.
□ Inspect dining, bar, kitchen, restrooms, office, storage, coolers, exterior, and hidden areas by the approved route.
□ Confirm no guest, employee, contractor, open flame, active cooking, leak, unsecured chemical, or immediate hazard
remains.
□ Check refrigeration, ventilation, doors, windows, gates, cash storage, keys, and systems that must remain on or off.
□ Document unresolved conditions and do not leave until responsibility is transferred.
□ Verify locks, alarms, keys, utilities, access, and final custody through physical or system confirmation.
• Beginning end-of-night building check without authorization, accountability, required contacts, and the current security
checklist.
• Sharing keys, codes, cards, or access information or leaving credentials unsecured.
• Locking, arming, or leaving without accounting for people and unresolved building conditions.
• Ignoring an alarm fault, lost credential, unsafe entry condition, or incomplete custody transfer.
• Failing to record time, employee, credential, result, exception, and final responsibility.

174. Alarm and Lock-Up SOP

SOP ID V1-174 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To arm alarms and lock the building only after completing the approved final check, confirming
authorized occupants, and documenting exceptions.
APPLIES TO: Authorized managers, keyholders, security employees, and designated leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The authorized manager completes the security sequence, controls credentials, and transfers
unresolved risk to the proper contact.
WHEN USED: After active service ends and before employees are released or the building is secured.
• Building security checklist
• Authorized keys, cards, codes, or alarm access
• Employee and visitor accountability
• Emergency and alarm contacts
• Security or access log
• Only authorized employees use keys, codes, locks, and alarm systems.
• People are accounted for before final locking, arming, or access transfer.
• Unresolved doors, alarms, utilities, hazards, or access losses are not ignored.
• Final checks, custody changes, and exceptions are documented.
1. Confirm authorization and obtain the checklist, keys, access devices, contacts, and accountability information for alarm and
lock-up.
2. Account for employees, guests, contractors, active transactions, and conditions that prevent secure closure or access control.
3. Use the approved route and sequence; do not enter or remain in an area that appears unsafe or unlawfully accessed.
4. Confirm the building check is complete and account for employees, guests, contractors, and authorized vehicles.
5. Lock interior controlled areas and exterior doors in the approved sequence while maintaining a safe final exit.
6. Arm the alarm with individual authorization and verify acceptance, zone status, and error messages.
7. Confirm final doors, record departure, and contact the alarm company or manager for unresolved faults.
8. Stop and contact management, alarm company, security, or emergency services when a condition cannot be resolved.
9. Verify locks, alarms, keys, utilities, access, and final custody through physical or system confirmation.
10. Record time, employee, credential, result, and exception before departure or transfer.

175. Key Control and Access Log SOP

SOP ID V1-175 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control keys and access credentials through authorized issue, secure custody, return tracking, loss
response, and manager review.
APPLIES TO: Authorized managers, keyholders, security employees, and designated leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The authorized manager completes the security sequence, controls credentials, and transfers
unresolved risk to the proper contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever key control and access log is required during opening, service, shift transition, or closing.
• Building security checklist
• Authorized keys, cards, codes, or alarm access
• Employee and visitor accountability
• Emergency and alarm contacts
• Security or access log
• Only authorized employees use keys, codes, locks, and alarm systems.
• People are accounted for before final locking, arming, or access transfer.
• Unresolved doors, alarms, utilities, hazards, or access losses are not ignored.
• Final checks, custody changes, and exceptions are documented.
1. Confirm authorization and obtain the checklist, keys, access devices, contacts, and accountability information for key control
and access log.
2. Account for employees, guests, contractors, active transactions, and conditions that prevent secure closure or access control.
3. Use the approved route and sequence; do not enter or remain in an area that appears unsafe or unlawfully accessed.
4. Issue access only with authorization and record person, credential identifier, access level, date, and purpose.
5. Prohibit copying, sharing, address-labeling, or unsecured storage of credentials.
6. Record temporary transfers and require return at role change, suspension, termination, or end of use.
7. Report loss or compromise, deactivate or change access, and complete periodic inventory.
8. Stop and contact management, alarm company, security, or emergency services when a condition cannot be resolved.
9. Verify locks, alarms, keys, utilities, access, and final custody through physical or system confirmation.
10. Record time, employee, credential, result, and exception before departure or transfer.


175. Key Control and Access Log SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The assigned keyholder confirms authority, people, access devices, security contacts, and unresolved conditions before
key control and access log.
□ Issue access only with authorization and record person, credential identifier, access level, date, and purpose.
□ Prohibit copying, sharing, address-labeling, or unsecured storage of credentials.
□ Record temporary transfers and require return at role change, suspension, termination, or end of use.
□ Report loss or compromise, deactivate or change access, and complete periodic inventory.
□ Verify locks, alarms, keys, utilities, access, and final custody through physical or system confirmation.
• Beginning key control and access log without authorization, accountability, required contacts, and the current security
checklist.
• Sharing keys, codes, cards, or access information or leaving credentials unsecured.
• Locking, arming, or leaving without accounting for people and unresolved building conditions.
• Ignoring an alarm fault, lost credential, unsafe entry condition, or incomplete custody transfer.
• Failing to record time, employee, credential, result, exception, and final responsibility.

OPERATIONAL GLOSSARY
Use these definitions as practical operating language for this SOP Library. They explain how the terms are used
inside The Gravity System; they do not replace applicable law, code, manufacturer instructions, professional advice,
or the restaurant's approved policies.
Approved Version - The current SOP version authorized for training and use. Superseded versions should be
removed from active use or clearly marked as retired.
Comp - A manager-authorized reduction or removal of a charge according to restaurant policy. The reason and
approval should be recorded when required.
Controlled Record - An operational record that should be completed accurately, stored securely, and retained
according to restaurant policy and applicable requirements.
Corrective Action - A documented response used to correct a failure, assign responsibility, reduce repeat risk, and
verify that the required follow-up was completed.
Critical Standards - The non-negotiable conditions that define safe, accurate, compliant, or acceptable
performance for the procedure.
Effective Date - The date an approved SOP becomes the restaurant's active operating standard.
Escalation Authority - The manager, owner, person in charge, or other authorized role that must make a decision
when the employee cannot safely or properly resolve an exception.
Exception - A condition that falls outside the approved procedure or limit and requires documentation, correction, or
management review.
Guest Recovery - An approved response used to address a service failure, protect the guest relationship, and
document any manager decision or follow-up that is required.
Manager Verification - Documented confirmation that the employee or process met the required standard during
actual operations, not only during explanation or classroom training.
Next Review Date - The scheduled date for management to confirm that the SOP is still accurate, usable, and
consistent with current operations and requirements.
Nonretaliation - The requirement that good-faith reporting or participation in an authorized review not be punished
or discouraged. Protected or formal matters should be routed to authorized leadership.


OPERATIONAL GLOSSARY - CONTINUED
Owner / SOP Owner - The person or role responsible for maintaining the SOP, routing revisions, and making sure
the current approved version remains available.
POS - Point-of-sale system used to enter orders, process payments, apply approved discounts or comps, and
produce transaction records.
Restaurant-Specific Customization - The fields that must be completed so a model SOP matches the restaurant's
actual equipment, policies, layout, authority, limits, records, and local requirements.
Revision Note - A reminder to record material changes in the restaurant master SOP index and retrain affected
employees when the approved process changes.
Service Recovery Authorization - The level of authority a manager or employee has to correct a guest problem,
issue an approved recovery, or escalate the decision.
Shift Handoff - A structured transfer of current conditions, unresolved issues, responsibilities, and required follow-
up from one employee or manager to the next.
SOP ID - The unique identification code assigned to a procedure so the restaurant can control versions, training,
approvals, and records without relying only on the title.
Training Sign-Off - The record showing who was trained, who trained or verified the employee, and when the
training occurred.
Training Verification - The internal readiness result used in this library: Not Ready, Retrain, or Approved. It
confirms restaurant training status only and is not a professional credential.
Version - The identifier used to distinguish the current approved SOP from earlier or later revisions.
Void - The approved cancellation of an item or transaction entry according to restaurant policy, with the required
reason and manager authorization recorded.
